  - S-7 (07:50:48) (ruling, Judge Tony Graf Jr.): "He walked with a gait or limp, his right leg stiff. He had nothing in his hands."
  - S-3 (02:24:10) (testimony, Agent Hull, former Utah State Bureau of Investigation agent (witness; Rev also renders Holt/Hall)): "there is no longer a visible limp or gait."

### C-11463 (ep 133)
- **Claim:** The host says the gun is supposed to be a German 98 Mauser family heirloom used in World War I and World War II that would take the shooter's shoulder out when fired.
- **Record:** Faumuina testified the rifle was a Mauser 98 (S-3 05:03:26), and the ruling finds a Mauser Model 98 bolt action refitted with a .30-06 barrel (S-7 07:48:35); the heirloom, war history and recoil details are not addressed.
  - S-3 (05:03:26) (testimony, Sgt. Jennifer Faumuina, Utah DPS State Bureau of Investigation (witness)): "That would be a rifle. So to be more specific, it was a Mauser 98 rifle."
  - S-7 (07:48:35) (ruling, Judge Tony Graf Jr.): "The rifle was a Mauser Model 98 bolt action that had been retrofitted to a 30-aught six barrel and was in operable condition. It contained an optical accessory."

### C-11489 (ep 133)
- **Claim:** The host says Donald Trump Jr. has been going into the courthouse with Erika Kirk, and says she would like his comment on the rooftop video.
- **Record:** The Deseret News (S-32) reports Erika Kirk was joined in court during the preliminary hearing week by allies including Donald Trump Jr., and KSL (S-20) places him in the courtroom gallery; the request for comment cannot be checked.
  - S-32 para 47 (tier2_reported, Deseret News): "including Donald Trump Jr., political commentator Jack Posobiec and Utah Sen. Mike Lee."
  - S-20 para 20 (tier2_reported, KSL): "Also present in the courtroom gallery on Monday was Donald Trump Jr."

### C-11536 (ep 134)
- **Claim:** In a Sky News clip the host plays, a reporter says that on 2025-09-11 at 5:27 PM Phoenix time Air Force 2 had landed about an hour earlier at Sky Harbor Airport carrying Kirk's body and that the motorcade was heading to Hansen Mortuary in Phoenix.
- **Record:** Photo captions (S-27, S-26) report that Erika Kirk left Air Force Two after accompanying Charlie Kirk's body, and that a motorcade brought the body to Hansen Mortuary Chapel in Phoenix on Sept. 11, 2025; the 5:27 PM timing and the one-hour gap are not covered.
  - S-27 para 187 (tier2_reported, The Republic/USA Today Network (photo caption)): "Erika Kirk waves as a motorcade brings the body of her husband, Charlie Kirk, to Hansen Mortuary Chapel in Phoenix Sept. 11, 2025."
  - S-26 para 32 (tier2_reported, Associated Press (photo caption)): "Erika Kirk (left) departs Air Force Two with Vice President JD Vance and his wife, Usha, after accompanying the body of Charlie Kirk on Thursday."

### C-11567 (ep 134)
- **Claim:** The host says her producer corrected her: the prosecution, not the defense, filed the memorandum with the autopsy injury excerpts.
- **Record:** The ruling records that the State filed its bindover memorandum on July 28, 2026, before the defense's Aug 11 briefing (S-7 07:42:28), confirming the producer's correction.
  - S-7 (07:42:28) (ruling, Judge Tony Graf Jr.): "The state filed its memorandum in support of a preliminary hearing bind over on July 28th, 2026. Defendant filed his preliminary hearing briefing on August 11th, 2026, and the state filed its reply memorandum on August 18th, 2026."

### C-11573 (ep 134)
- **Claim:** The host says Erika has said that Charlie died instantly.
- **Record:** Fox News (S-44) quotes Erika Kirk: "he died instantaneously... He died on the scene."
  - S-44 para 174 (tier2_attributed, Erika Kirk): ""The way the bullet hit him, he died instantaneously," Kirk explained. "He died on the scene."

### C-11588 (ep 134)
- **Claim:** The host says Tyler Robinson is up for the death penalty.
- **Record:** Judge Graf stated at the Sept 1 bindover (S-7, 01:42:40) that Robinson is charged with capital aggravated murder and the State is seeking the death penalty; Jeff Gray announced the notice of intent (S-11, 04:36).
  - S-7 (01:42:40) (ruling, Judge Tony Graf Jr.): "The defendant is charged with aggravated murder, a capital offense for which the state of Utah is seeking the death penalty"
  - S-11 (04:36) (official_statement, Jeff Gray, Utah County Attorney): "following the press conference, I am filing a notice of intent to seek the death penalty."

### C-11596 (ep 135)
- **Claim:** The host says the person who signed off on Charlie Kirk's autopsy was Dr. Andrew Guajardo.
- **Record:** Agent Hull testified the autopsy was performed by Dr. Guajardo (S-2, 06:00:30); the first name is not stated in the record.
  - S-2 (06:00:30) (testimony, SBI Agent David Hull (witness)): "Dr. Guajardo, I believe is how it's pronounced."

### C-11602 (ep 135)
- **Claim:** The host reads on air messages said to be from Tyler Robinson to gamer friends on Discord saying it was him at UVU, that he is surrendering through a sheriff friend, and thanking them for the good times.
- **Record:** The bindover ruling quotes the Sept 11 Discord message 'I'm sorry for all of this. I'm surrendering through a sheriff friend in a few moments' (S-7, 07:55:06), and Exhibit 16.2 (Discord with gaming friends) is quoted as 'It was me at UVU yesterday' (S-7, 03:58:00).
  - S-7 (07:55:06) (ruling, Judge Tony Graf Jr.): "I'm sorry for all of this. I'm surrendering through a sheriff friend in a few moments."
  - S-7 (03:58:00) (argument, prosecutor Ryan McBride, Utah County Attorney's Office): "he also claimed credit to all of his friends on the Discord app. Exhibit 16.2, you have seen this. "It was me at UVU yesterday.""
  - S-7 (02:29:28) (exhibit, prosecutor Ryan McBride): "This is communications between the defendant and his gaming friends on the Discord chat"

### C-11618 (ep 135)
- **Claim:** The host says Lance Twiggs testified at the preliminary hearing that he goes by Luna, which she suspects is a gaming character he engages with outside of Lance Twiggs.
- **Record:** In his sworn recorded statement played at the preliminary hearing, Twiggs said he went by Luna 'to some people, yeah, but not as an overall thing' (S-5, 02:32:01).
  - S-5 (02:31:56) (exhibit, interviewer, Utah County Attorney's Office (State's Exhibit 16 recording)): "And I understand you were going by Luna at some point?"
  - S-5 (02:32:01) (exhibit, Lance Twiggs, Robinson's roommate/partner (recorded interview, State's Exhibit 16)): "To some people, yeah, but not as an overall thing."

### C-11624 (ep 135)
- **Claim:** The host says Brian Harpole's account is that he did all of the work while Rick Cutler was holding him and Frank Turek was praying in the back.
- **Record:** KSL (S-58) reports that in Harpole's account Rick Cutler held his shirt while he gave first aid and Turek prayed out loud; 'doing all of the work' is the host's characterisation.
  - S-58 para 19 (tier2_attributed, Brian Harpole): "another private security operator, Rick Cutler, held onto Harpole’s shirt to keep him from falling out of the van"
  - S-58 para 19 (tier2_attributed, Brian Harpole): "Meanwhile, Turek prayed out loud."

### C-11631 (ep 135)
- **Claim:** In a clip the host plays, Brian Harpole says he cut Charlie Kirk's shirt off, then went back and forth with the medical staff on drugs, then exited the room and guarded the door.
- **Record:** KSL (S-58) and the Salt Lake Tribune (S-59) report Harpole said he cut Kirk's shirt off, tried to help the medical staff, and then stood guard at the door; the back-and-forth about drugs is not specifically covered.
  - S-58 para 21 (tier2_attributed, Brian Harpole): "Then Harpole said he cut Kirk’s Freedom T-shirt off to prep him for a defibrillator."
  - S-59 para 28 (tier2_attributed, Brian Harpole): "When they arrived at the Timpanogos Regional Hospital, Harpole remembered placing Kirk on a gurney and trying to help medical staff there. Then, he stood guard at the door."

### C-11652 (ep 135)
- **Claim:** The host says she thinks the ATF testified that it received five fragments, four of which were unusable and one big enough to use.
- **Record:** Karner testified she received one bullet jacket fragment and four lead fragments (S-5, 06:25:53), and the ATF report found the four lead fragments not suitable for microscopic comparison (S-5, 05:31:54).
  - S-5 (06:25:53) (testimony, Samantha Karner, ATF firearm and toolmark examiner (defense witness)): "I received a firearm, cartridge case, three cartridges, one bullet jacket fragment, and four lead fragments."
  - S-5 (05:31:54) (exhibit, Sgt. Faumuina, Utah State Bureau of Investigation (witness)): ""The Exhibit 6B through 6E lead fragments are not suitable for microscopic comparison.""

### C-11653 (ep 135)
- **Claim:** Per the anesthesiologist's email, no ambulance with resuscitative equipment or trained personnel was immediately available at the scene (no defibrillators, no access to IV drugs), and Charlie's neck should have been stabilized before moving him.
- **Record:** The UVU After-Action Report finds no ambulance was staged on site for the September 10 event (S-12, p. 59); the equipment and neck-stabilisation points are not addressed.
  - S-12 p. 59 (report_finding, Utah Valley University After-Action Report (Project Team)): "No ambulance was staged on site for the September 10 TPUSA event."

### C-11666 (ep 135)
- **Claim:** The host says it has been proven true that the person on the rooftop was military crawling, and that the witness saw the person get down into a position before the bang.
- **Record:** Officer Bagley testified dispatch described the rooftop male running to the edge, dropping down, crawling and getting into a prone position (S-2, 02:34:29).
  - S-2 (02:34:29) (testimony, Officer Bagley, UVU Police (witness)): "They stated that he ran to the edge, dropped down, crawled, got in a prone position."

### C-11670 (ep 135)
- **Claim:** The host says the weapon at issue is a German 98 Mauser, which she says would be hard to conceal in the way a handgun could.
- **Record:** Sgt. Faumuina testified the recovered gun was a Mauser 98 rifle (S-3, 05:03:26).
  - S-3 (05:03:26) (testimony, Sgt. Jennifer Faumuina, Utah DPS State Bureau of Investigation (witness)): "That would be a rifle. So to be more specific, it was a Mauser 98 rifle."

### C-11683 (ep 136)
- **Claim:** The host says Harpole tells a story that a nurse wiped the blood off his face, which she uses to argue he had no reason to stop and put on gloves in the moving car.
- **Record:** KSL (S-58) and the Salt Lake Tribune (S-59) report Harpole said a nurse washed the blood off his face; the host's argument about gloves is her own inference.
  - S-58 para 21 (tier2_attributed, Brian Harpole): "A nurse approached him, took him to a room and physically washed the blood off his face and hands."
  - S-59 para 29 (tier2_attributed, Brian Harpole): "When he finally stepped away, Harpole said, a nurse helped him wash the blood off his face and arms."

### C-11684 (ep 136)
- **Claim:** The host recounts being told that Rick Cutler did nothing but hold Brian Harpole with two hands so he would not fall out of the car, and asks whether Cutler would need gloves for that, using a diagram.
- **Record:** KSL (S-58) reports Harpole said Rick Cutler held onto his shirt to keep him from falling out of the van; the gloves question is the host's argument.
  - S-58 para 19 (tier2_attributed, Brian Harpole): "another private security operator, Rick Cutler, held onto Harpole’s shirt to keep him from falling out of the van"

### C-11690 (ep 136) · keeps revised-by-host
- **Claim:** The host says the screwdriver found on the rooftop has Lance Twiggs's DNA alongside Tyler Robinson's DNA.
- **Record:** The FBI report interprets the screwdriver DNA (Item 8.1) as from two individuals, one of whom is Twiggs (S-3 05:11:41), with results 30 quintillion times more likely if Twiggs and Tyler Robinson are contributors (S-7 06:18:43).
  - S-3 (05:11:41) (exhibit, Sgt. Jennifer Faumuina, Utah DPS State Bureau of Investigation (witness)): ""Male DNA was obtained from Item 8, sub 1. Item 8, sub 1 was interpreted as originating from two individuals, one of whom is Twiggs."
  - S-7 (06:18:43) (exhibit, FBI report (State's Exhibit 31), read by prosecutor Chad Grunander): "Turning to the screwdriver, the DNA results from this item are 30 quintillion times more likely if Twiggs and Tyler Robinson are contributors than if Twiggs and an unknown unrelated person are contributors."

### C-11692 (ep 136)
- **Claim:** In a preliminary-hearing clip the host plays, Officer Bagley testifies that he did not know exactly who the plainclothes individual wearing a badge and carrying a handgun who accompanied him up the Losi building stairs was, nor what agency he was from or where he was from.
- **Record:** Bagley testified he did not know exactly who the badge-wearing plainclothes backup on the Losee stairs was, nor his agency, and that the man carried a handgun (S-2, 04:08:45-04:09:00).
  - S-2 (04:08:45) (testimony, Officer Bagley, UVU Police (witness)): "I don't know who he was exactly. He had a badge on and I had somebody with me to back me up as I ran up the stairs"
  - S-2 (04:08:56) (testimony, Officer Bagley, UVU Police (witness)): "No."
  - S-2 (04:09:00) (testimony, Officer Bagley, UVU Police (witness)): "A handgun, yes."

### C-11693 (ep 136)
- **Claim:** In a preliminary-hearing clip the host plays, Officer Bagley, under questioning, confirms that the second individual who accompanied him onto the Losi rooftop appeared to have a badge and was carrying a handgun.
- **Record:** Bagley testified the plainclothes man backing him up had a badge on and carried a handgun (S-2, 04:08:45, 04:09:00).
  - S-2 (04:08:45) (testimony, Officer Bagley, UVU Police (witness)): "I don't know who he was exactly. He had a badge on and I had somebody with me to back me up as I ran up the stairs"
  - S-2 (04:09:00) (testimony, Officer Bagley, UVU Police (witness)): "A handgun, yes."

### C-11694 (ep 136)
- **Claim:** In a preliminary-hearing clip the host plays, a questioner describes Officer Bagley's body cam as 27 minutes 35 seconds, starting at the Hall of Flags and ending while he was still on the roof before crime tape was up, and Bagley agrees it is his only body cam footage that day.
- **Record:** Defense counsel described the 27:35 body cam running from the Hall of Flags to the roof before the tape went up, and Bagley agreed it was his only body cam that day (S-2, 04:12:40-04:12:54).
  - S-2 (04:12:40) (exhibit, defense attorney Kathryn Nester): "So that 27 minute and 35 second body cam that starts when you're at the Hall of Flags and ends while you're still on the roof but haven't put the crime tape up yet"
  - S-2 (04:12:54) (testimony, Officer Bagley, UVU Police (witness)): "Yes."

### C-11695 (ep 136)
- **Claim:** The host says Bagley testifies he reached Losi at 12:44 and discovered the screwdriver 10 to 15 feet past the railing in the gravel.
- **Record:** Bagley testified he reached the Losee rooftop at 12:44 (S-2, 01:43:05) and marked the screwdriver about 10 to 15 feet past the guardrail (S-2, 02:13:47).
  - S-2 (01:43:05) (testimony, Officer Bagley, UVU Police (witness; Rev label 'Richard Novak')): "I got up there 12:44."
  - S-2 (02:13:47) (testimony, Officer Bagley, UVU Police (witness; Rev label 'Richard Novak')): "Here's the guardrail in the center of the picture with the red brick. Just over that, about 10, 15 feet in, about right there."

### C-11696 (ep 136)
- **Claim:** Per a Kash Patel public statement played in the episode, the first FBI agents arrived at UVU with chiefs of police at 12:39 PM, 16 minutes after the 12:23 PM shooting, and secured the scene.
- **Record:** CBS News (S-52) reports FBI agents and police chiefs arrived at the scene within 16 minutes of the shooting.
  - S-52 para 128 (tier2_reported, CBS News): "FBI agents and chiefs of police arrived at the scene within 16 minutes of the shooting."

### C-11786 (ep 137)
- **Claim:** During cross-examination, Officer Christopher Bagley testified that he did not review the police department video footage to determine whether anyone else had been on the rooftop between the suspect's jump and his own arrival.
- **Record:** On cross Bagley answered 'No' when asked whether he reviewed the video for anyone on or disturbing the roof between the jump and his arrival (S-2, 04:10:46).
  - S-2 (04:10:46) (testimony, Officer Bagley, UVU Police (witness)): "No."

### C-11787 (ep 137)
- **Claim:** In the played testimony, Officer Bagley says people were up on the roof looking for an individual who ran north, and that it was not until he went to the police department and saw the video that he knew the individual had jumped off the building.
- **Record:** Bagley testified people were up there looking for an individual who ran that way, and only after seeing the video at the PD did he know the man had jumped off (S-2, 04:09:47).
  - S-2 (04:09:47) (testimony, Officer Bagley, UVU Police (witness)): "There was people up there looking for an individual that ran that way"
  - S-2 (04:09:47) (testimony, Officer Bagley, UVU Police (witness)): "it wasn't until I went to the PD and I actually saw him jump off of the building that I knew he was off the building."

### C-11788 (ep 137)
- **Claim:** In the played testimony, Bagley answers 'I didn't notice anybody' when asked whether he checked for anyone on the roof between the suspect's jump and his arrival, and the defense attorney then puts to him that he did not review the video to see whether anybody had been on or disturbed the scene.
- **Record:** Defense counsel put to Bagley that he did not review the video for anyone on or disturbing the scene between the jump and his arrival, and he answered 'No' (S-2, 04:10:46).
  - S-2 (04:10:46) (testimony, Officer Bagley, UVU Police (witness)): "No."

### C-11835 (ep 138)
- **Claim:** The host says the claimed LGBTQ motive collapsed because Lance testified he had never spoken to Tyler about LGBTQ issues, and that this erases the political motive.
- **Record:** In his sworn recorded statement Twiggs said he and Robinson did 'not really' discuss gender identity or LGBTQ rights (S-5, 02:31:12); the motive conclusion is the host's opinion.
  - S-5 (02:31:12) (exhibit, Lance Twiggs, Robinson's roommate/partner (recorded interview, State's Exhibit 16)): "No, not really. Usually, if he did talk about politics stuff, it was relating to Trump or current policies being issued or voted on"

### C-11836 (ep 138)
- **Claim:** The host says Lance testified that Tyler Robinson had never mentioned the name Charlie Kirk to him, and asks where the motive is now.
- **Record:** Twiggs said he had never personally heard Robinson talk about Charlie Kirk specifically (S-5, 02:30:54).
  - S-5 (02:30:54) (exhibit, Lance Twiggs, Robinson's roommate/partner (recorded interview, State's Exhibit 16)): "I personally had never heard him talk about Charlie Kirk before specifically."

### C-11839 (ep 138)
- **Claim:** The host says it is a lie that Tyler Robinson's gun was officially tied to the bullet fragment recovered in Charlie Kirk's autopsy, citing the ATF testimony at the preliminary hearing that gave a range for the metal fragments.
- **Record:** The ATF report read at the hearing says the Exhibit 6 autopsy bullet jacket fragment 'cannot be identified or excluded as having been fired from the Exhibit 1 rifle' (S-5 05:31:18), and Karner explained she gave a diameter range because of damage (S-5 06:45:07).
  - S-5 (05:31:18) (exhibit, Sgt. Faumuina, Utah State Bureau of Investigation (witness)): ""The Exhibit 6 bullet jacket fragment cannot be identified or excluded as having been fired from the Exhibit 1 rifle"
  - S-5 (06:45:07) (testimony, Samantha Karner, ATF firearm and toolmark examiner (defense witness)): "The reason I provided a range was because of the damage and deformity to the base. So, at times, it wasn't a perfect circle"

### C-11848 (ep 138)
- **Claim:** The host says the phone of Lance (referenced in the Tyler Robinson preliminary-hearing record) was photographed by police with damage in the top-left corner of the screen at the location where the device's status-bar timestamp would appear.
- **Record:** Agent Davis testified Twiggs's phone was photographed at his first interview (S-5 02:36:28) and that Twiggs identified it by the large crack in the upper left of the screen (S-5 02:38:12); the timestamp-location point is not addressed.
  - S-5 (02:36:28) (testimony, Agent Davis, SBI (witness)): "In Lance's first interview, he's showing the FBI agents these text messages, describes them to the agents. And at that time, his phone was photographed."
  - S-5 (02:38:12) (testimony, Agent Davis, SBI (witness)): "The second interview, he refers to the large crack in the upper left screen. So he identifies that from his second interview that was given on April 20th."

### C-11850 (ep 138)
- **Claim:** The host says the messages were presented without any timestamps and that it is unknown why.
- **Record:** AP (S-33) reports the texts shared in court documents have no timestamps, so it is unclear when Robinson sent them.
  - S-33 para 57 (tier2_reported, Associated Press): "The texts shared in court documents do not have timestamps"

### C-11945 (ep 139)
- **Claim:** The host says the 30-06 bullet did not go through Charlie Kirk and did not almost decapitate him as would have been expected.
- **Record:** Judge Graf found the bullet traveled front to back and downward and 'did not exit his body' (S-7 08:16:02); the 'expected decapitation' remark is the host's opinion.
  - S-7 (08:16:02) (ruling, Judge Tony Graf Jr.): "the bullet traveled anatomically from front to back and downward after striking Mr. Kirk. The projectile did not exit his body."

### C-11961 (ep 139)
- **Claim:** The host says she learned that the Dremel used to carve the bullets belonged to Lance Twiggs.
- **Record:** The ATF's Caitlin Oliver testified (S-32) that the engraving tool belonged to Twiggs, and KSL (S-40) reports Twiggs said Robinson asked to use his tool to engrave bullets.
  - S-32 para 110 (tier2_attributed, Caitlin Oliver): "The tool belonged to Twiggs, and his DNA was found on the tool’s buttons, Oliver said."
  - S-40 para 24 (tier2_attributed, Lance Twiggs): "Robinson told Twiggs he was going on a hunting trip with his family and wanted to use a tool belonging to Twiggs to engrave bullets, he said in the interview."

### C-11974 (ep 139)
- **Claim:** The host says Lance claims he found a handwritten note under his keyboard, took a picture of it, and then burned the note to hide the evidence, which she notes would not hide the evidence.
- **Record:** Court documents (S-17) say the roommate found the note under the keyboard, and KSL (S-40) reports Twiggs photographed it before burning it, which answers the earlier rejection's point about the burning; the motive and the host's comment are not covered.
  - S-17 para 104 (tier2_attributed, court documents): "The roommate found a note under the keyboard that read, "I had the opportunity to take out Charlie Kirk and I'm going to take it," the court documents state."
  - S-40 para 25 (tier2_reported, KSL): "Twiggs, whom Robinson referred to as Luna, took a picture of the note before burning it. Investigators retrieved the picture of the note after seizing Twiggs' phone and downloading the data."

### C-12024 (ep 140)
- **Claim:** The host says Tyler Robinson allegedly wrote in the confession messages to Lance that he had hoped to keep the secret until he died of old age, which she says undercuts the suicide narrative.
- **Record:** Agent Davis read the text 'To be honest, I had hoped to keep this secret till I died of old age' (S-5, 02:42:39), also quoted in the Information (S-1, p. 8).
  - S-5 (02:42:39) (exhibit, Agent Davis, SBI (witness)): "To be honest, I had hoped to keep this secret till I died of old age. I am sorry to involve you.""
  - S-1 p. 8 (charging_allegation, Utah County Attorney's Office): "To be honest I had hoped to keep this secret till I died of old age. I am sorry to involve you."

### C-12039 (ep 140)
- **Claim:** The host says that according to police Robinson went to Chick-fil-A on campus, and that no footage of this has been shown or explained.
- **Record:** Agent Hull testified Robinson bought and ate food at the Chick-fil-A in the Sorensen Center (S-3, 00:48:15), as the bindover ruling also found (S-7, 07:49:47).
  - S-3 (00:48:15) (testimony, SBI Agent Hull (witness)): "He went to Chick-fil-A in the Sorenson building and purchased some food, sat and ate the food."
  - S-7 (07:49:47) (ruling, Judge Tony Graf Jr.): "Video surveillance captured a person identified as Mr. Robinson returning to campus on foot from the northeast just after 10:00 AM. He ate at Chick-fil-A in the Sorenson Center, one of the buildings adjacent to the outdoor amphitheater."

### C-12040 (ep 140)
- **Claim:** The host recounts the official account that after leaving campus Robinson parked off campus, changed outfits, and put on a long-sleeve black shirt and blue jeans before returning to commit the shooting.
- **Record:** The ruling finds Robinson returned at 11:53 a.m. in a dark long-sleeved shirt and jeans (S-7, 07:50:48) and cites texts saying he changed outfits (S-7, 08:24:13).
  - S-7 (07:50:48) (ruling, Judge Tony Graf Jr.): "At 11:53 AM, a person identified as Mr. Robinson then returned to campus wearing the same shoes, but different clothing, a dark long sleeved shirt, jeans, and a backpack."
  - S-7 (08:24:13) (ruling, Judge Tony Graf Jr.): "text messages attributed to him in which he stated he had concealed his rifle by leaving it in a bush where he changed outfits."

### C-12042 (ep 140)
- **Claim:** The host recounts the official account that Robinson shoved a broken-down German 98 Mauser into his pants and limped back onto campus with a new hat and new backpack.
- **Record:** The State argued Robinson returned at 11:54 in a black shirt and long pants to obscure the rifle concealed down his pants (S-7, 03:48:26), and the rifle was a Mauser 98 (S-3, 05:03:26); hat, backpack and limp details are not addressed.
  - S-7 (03:48:26) (argument, prosecutor Ryan McBride): "The defendant then returned at 11:54 AM, this time dressed to kill. Black shirt, long pants to obscure the rifle he had concealed down his pants"
  - S-3 (05:03:26) (testimony, Sgt. Jennifer Faumuina, Utah DPS State Bureau of Investigation (witness)): "That would be a rifle. So to be more specific, it was a Mauser 98 rifle."

### C-12043 (ep 140)
- **Claim:** The host recounts that after the shooting Robinson changed back into his maroon shirt while keeping the jeans on and was then spotted at Dairy Queen.
- **Record:** The bindover ruling finds Robinson returned at 11:53 AM in a dark long-sleeved shirt and jeans (S-7 07:50:48) and arrived at the sheriff's office around 9 PM in a maroon shirt and jeans (S-7 07:56:00); Dairy Queen is not in the record.
  - S-7 (07:50:48) (ruling, Judge Tony Graf Jr.): "At 11:53 AM, a person identified as Mr. Robinson then returned to campus wearing the same shoes, but different clothing, a dark long sleeved shirt, jeans, and a backpack."
  - S-7 (07:56:00) (ruling, Judge Tony Graf Jr.): "At approximately 9:00 PM, Mr. Robinson arrived at the Washington County Sheriff's Office. He was wearing a maroon shirt and jeans."

### C-12045 (ep 140)
- **Claim:** The host recounts that after the steak Robinson drove back up to Orem, another three hours, to retrieve his rifle during the largest federal manhunt, returning to the scene of the crime.
- **Record:** Robinson's text read 'Going to attempt to retrieve it again' (S-5, 02:43:32), and the State described him going back to retrieve the rifle unsuccessfully (S-7, 06:11:51).
  - S-5 (02:43:32) (exhibit, Agent Davis, SBI (witness)): "If I'm able to grab my rifle unseen, I will have left no evidence. Going to attempt to retrieve it again."
  - S-7 (06:11:51) (argument, prosecutor Chad Grunander): "he hid it and he went back to try and retrieve it and was unsuccessful."

### C-12047 (ep 140)
- **Claim:** According to Agent Hull's testimony as played in the episode, the suspect vehicle "made contact with an officer who was doing security detail," identified as Officer Goforth.
- **Record:** Agent Hull testified the vehicle made contact with an officer doing security detail, Officer Goforth (S-3, 00:57:54-00:58:01).
  - S-3 (00:57:54) (testimony, SBI Agent Hull (witness)): "When that vehicle was stopped, it actually made contact with an officer who was doing security detail."
  - S-3 (00:58:01) (testimony, SBI Agent Hull (witness)): "Officer Goforth."

### C-12048 (ep 140)
- **Claim:** In the testimony audio the host plays, Agent Hull says the vehicle is now pulling away and heading east on 800 South.
- **Record:** Agent Hull testified 'That vehicle's now pulling away and heading east on 800 South' (S-3, 00:57:42).
  - S-3 (00:57:42) (testimony, SBI Agent Hull (witness)): "That vehicle's now pulling away and heading east on 800 South."

### C-12054 (ep 140)
- **Claim:** The host says Robinson had not changed his shirt after returning home because he was going to be wearing the same shirt when he was arrested.
- **Record:** The ruling finds Robinson wore a maroon short-sleeve shirt that morning (S-7, 07:49:47) and a maroon shirt when he arrived at the Washington County Sheriff's Office on September 11 (S-7, 07:56:00).
  - S-7 (07:49:47) (ruling, Judge Tony Graf Jr.): "A person identified as Mr. Robinson exited the Charger and walked on foot out of the parking garage toward the outdoor amphitheater on campus. He was wearing a maroon short sleeve shirt, shorts, a hat, and a backpack."
  - S-7 (07:56:00) (ruling, Judge Tony Graf Jr.): "At approximately 9:00 PM, Mr. Robinson arrived at the Washington County Sheriff's Office. He was wearing a maroon shirt and jeans."

### C-12057 (ep 140)
- **Claim:** The host says George Zinn, whom she calls decoy boy number one, said "I did it. I did it." before coming down, and that she admits she did not pay enough attention to his arrest.
- **Record:** The AAR records George Zinn taken into custody at 12:26 p.m. after allegedly admitting involvement, later determined not a suspect (S-12, p. 21).
  - S-12 p. 21 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report (Lake Forest Group / Brownstein Hyatt Farber Schreck)): "12:26 p.m. UVUPD reported a suspect, George Zinn, an event attendee, was in custody after allegedly admitting involvement in the shooting. It was later determined he was not a suspect."

### C-12152 (ep 141)
- **Claim:** The host says the preliminary hearing repeatedly discussed Utah's Rule 1102, under which hearsay is allowed at that stage as probative, and that this lets people infer from the evidence outside the courtroom too.
- **Record:** Judge Graf noted reliable hearsay is allowed at a preliminary hearing under the Utah Constitution and Rule of Evidence 1102 (S-3, 03:23:26).
  - S-3 (03:23:26) (ruling, Judge Tony Graf Jr.): "reliable hearsay is allowed in a preliminary hearing under the Utah Constitution and under the Rules of Evidence 1102."

### C-12156 (ep 141)
- **Claim:** In a played exchange, the host cites that Tyler Robinson stopped and ate at Chick-fil-A as, in her view, an odd lunch before killing Charlie Kirk over LGBTQ issues. The transcript does not name Robinson in the line itself.
- **Record:** Agent Hull testified Robinson bought and ate food at the Sorensen Center Chick-fil-A (S-3, 00:48:15), as the ruling found (S-7, 07:49:47).
  - S-3 (00:48:15) (testimony, SBI Agent Hull (witness)): "He went to Chick-fil-A in the Sorenson building and purchased some food, sat and ate the food."
  - S-7 (07:49:47) (ruling, Judge Tony Graf Jr.): "Video surveillance captured a person identified as Mr. Robinson returning to campus on foot from the northeast just after 10:00 AM. He ate at Chick-fil-A in the Sorenson Center, one of the buildings adjacent to the outdoor amphitheater."

### C-12173 (ep 141)
- **Claim:** In a played exchange, the host says aggravated murder is narrowly defined and carries a death sentence, which is why it matters that Wilson could not name it.
- **Record:** The County Attorney's release states aggravated murder carries death, life without parole or 25 years to life (S-10, p. 1), and the State is seeking death here (S-7, 01:42:40).
  - S-10 p. 1 (official_statement, Utah County Attorney's Office (Jeff Gray)): "aggravated murder carries a penalty of either death, life in prison without the possibility of parole, or twenty-five years to life"
  - S-7 (01:42:40) (ruling, Judge Tony Graf Jr.): "The defendant is charged with aggravated murder, a capital offense for which the state of Utah is seeking the death penalty"

### C-12174 (ep 141)
- **Claim:** In a played exchange, the host lists the seven charges as aggravated murder, felony discharge of a weapon, two counts of obstruction of justice, two counts of witness tampering, and a violence-in-front-of-a-minor charge.
- **Record:** The bindover ruling lists aggravated murder, felony discharge of a firearm, two counts of obstruction, two counts of witness tampering and a violent offense in the presence of a child (S-7, 07:41:21).
  - S-7 (07:41:21) (ruling, Judge Tony Graf Jr.): "count one, aggravated murder, a capital offense, together with the state's victim targeting notice. Count two, felony discharge of a firearm causing serious bodily injury, a first degree felony, counts three and four, obstruction of justice, second degree felonies"
  - S-7 (07:41:21) (ruling, Judge Tony Graf Jr.): "counts five and six, tampering with a witness, third degree felonies, and count seven, a violent offense committed in the presence of a child, a class B misdemeanor, which the state alleges is reclassified as a class A misdemeanor"

### C-12177 (ep 141)
- **Claim:** The host says the server gave a first-person account of serving him, that they contacted the FBI, and that he shared his experience, recognized Tyler Robinson on the news, and gave an interview; she adds that she spoke to the owner.
- **Record:** The Panguitch restaurant owner told Fox News (S-65) that a server recognized Robinson's picture and the tip went to the FBI, which interviewed the owner and two servers; a first-person media account from the server is not covered.
  - S-65 para 168 (tier2_attributed, unnamed Panguitch restaurant owner): "I had a server say that they were relatively certain that they had had that person in that night — late,"
  - S-65 para 169 (tier2_reported, Fox News): "The FBI called him back and asked for any information on the lone diner. Investigators interviewed the owner and two servers."

### C-12178 (ep 141)
- **Claim:** The host says the lead investigator testified at the preliminary hearing that he identified the person in a maroon shirt on the stairs as the same person later in a black shirt because both wore Converse sneakers, and that Wilson called that compelling and tweeted it as part of the overwhelming evidence.
- **Record:** Lead agent Hull testified Robinson wore a maroon T-shirt and Converse shoes (S-3 00:47:48) and, asked why the returning person in different clothing was Robinson, said 'The shoes are the same ... it's the same person' (S-3 00:51:25); the Wilson part is outside the record.
  - S-3 (00:51:25) (testimony, SBI Agent Hull (witness)): "The shoes are the same and the images that we have from my viewing and seeing Mr. Robinson on the video, it's the same person."
  - S-3 (00:47:48) (testimony, SBI Agent Hull (witness)): "It's a maroon color T-shirt with gray or olive colored shorts and then converse shoes."

### C-12216 (ep 142)
- **Claim:** Per Terryl Farnsworth's 1102 statement, which the host shows and reads, he provided SBI with four videos through a Google Drive on September 10, 2025.
- **Record:** Agent Hull testified Visual Impulse provided four videos in total (S-3, 02:01:52); the Google Drive delivery is not addressed.
  - S-3 (02:01:52) (testimony, Agent Hull, former Utah State Bureau of Investigation agent (witness; Rev also renders Holt/Hall)): "I believe there were four total videos that encompassed everything that they provided to us."

### C-12226 (ep 142)
- **Claim:** SBI Agent David Hull testified that Farnsworth originally provided, he believed, a flash drive to Lieutenant O'Brien, then downloadable electronic versions to Agent Mortonson on September 10, with the 1102 collected later on May 6, 2026 – not the SD card. The host describes these as Google links.
- **Record:** Agent Hull testified Farnsworth originally gave a flash drive to Lt. O'Brien, electronic versions to Agent Mortensen on September 10, and an 1102 was collected May 6, 2026 (S-2, 05:26:19).
  - S-2 (05:26:19) (testimony, SBI Agent David Hull (lead case agent, witness)): "Mr. Farnsworth originally provided, I believe, a flash drive to Lieutenant O'Brien with one of the local agencies"
  - S-2 (05:26:19) (testimony, SBI Agent David Hull (lead case agent, witness)): "a short time after that on September 10th, he provided downloadable versions, electronic versions to Agent Mortenson, and an 1102 was collected from him on May 6th of 2026."

### C-12246 (ep 142)
- **Claim:** The host says defense attorney Kathy Nester (transcribed Netor) asked questions and asserted that the defense does not know whether the footage was edited.
- **Record:** Defense attorney Kathryn Nester argued that someone prepared the Visual Impulse clips, 'We don't know who,' and the defense could not cross-examine 'in terms of what, if anything, was edited' (S-2 05:36:00).
  - S-2 (05:36:00) (argument, defense attorney Kathryn Nester): "someone prepared these clips. We don't know who. We don't have that person here to cross-examine in terms of what, if anything, was edited."

### C-12262 (ep 142)
- **Claim:** The host says Erika Kirk described Charlie as so excited to do what he always does, so excited to go to UVU, that he couldn't sleep.
- **Record:** Erika Kirk is reported (S-26) as recalling that on his last night Charlie was too excited to sleep.
  - S-26 para 33 (tier2_attributed, Erika Kirk): "on what turned out to be the last night of Charlie Kirk, her husband, he was too excited to sleep."

### C-12304 (ep 143)
- **Claim:** The host reads the Community Note, which says preliminary hearings decide only probable cause, reliable hearsay is allowed under the Utah 1102 standard, and defense counsel often skip alternative theories to avoid previewing strategy, so silence does not prove they lack merit.
- **Record:** Judge Graf stated the preliminary-hearing standard is probable cause (S-4, 09:51) and that reliable hearsay is allowed under Rule 1102 (S-3, 03:23:26).
  - S-4 (09:51) (ruling, Judge Tony Graf Jr.): "The standard at a preliminary hearing is probable cause."
  - S-3 (03:23:26) (ruling, Judge Tony Graf Jr.): "reliable hearsay is allowed in a preliminary hearing under the Utah Constitution and under the Rules of Evidence 1102."

  - S-66 para 20 (tier2_reported, The New York Times): "The lawsuit, filed in federal court in Nashville, Tennessee"
  - S-66 para 12 (tier2_reported, The New York Times): "In the 69-page lawsuit, Harpole, who owns a private security firm and worked for Kirk for several years"

  - S-3 (01:41:38) (testimony, Agent Hull, former Utah State Bureau of Investigation agent (witness; Rev also renders Holt/Hall)): "I believe it was approximately an hour after, so what would've been approximately 2:30."

### C-12382 (ep 144)

  - S-2 (04:32:57) (testimony, SBI Agent David Hull (lead case agent, witness)): "Sergeant Mark Bricker was put in touch with UVU's surveillance and operations team to start working through any available footage or information that we had that had been recorded."

### C-12384 (ep 144)

  - S-2 (06:42:37) (testimony, SBI Agent David Hull (witness)): "Utah County Attorney's Office. I believe Ms. Derfler is the one that produced the video."

  - S-65 para 169 (tier2_reported, Fox News): "The FBI called him back and asked for any information on the lone diner. Investigators interviewed the owner and two servers."

- **Claim:** The host says that Charlie was not sleeping the night before and that Erika Kirk says this was because he was so excited about the UVU event, which the host doubts.

  - S-26 para 33 (tier2_attributed, Erika Kirk): "on what turned out to be the last night of Charlie Kirk, her husband, he was too excited to sleep."

  - S-48 para 165 (tier2_reported, Fox News): "Hibbs reached out asking if he could get his brother entry into the Utah event."

  - S-7 (07:41:21) (ruling, Judge Tony Graf Jr.): "The court acting as a magistrate conducted a five-day preliminary hearing from July 6th to July 10th of this year."

### C-12601 (ep 146)
- **Claim:** The host says the preliminary hearing took place across five days in early July.
- **Record:** The ruling states the court conducted a five-day preliminary hearing from July 6 to July 10 (S-7, 07:41:21).
  - S-7 (07:41:21) (ruling, Judge Tony Graf Jr.): "The court acting as a magistrate conducted a five-day preliminary hearing from July 6th to July 10th of this year."

### C-12602 (ep 146)
- **Claim:** The host says she told viewers that under Utah's 1102 rule, reliable hearsay is permitted at the preliminary hearing stage.
- **Record:** Judge Graf noted reliable hearsay is allowed at a preliminary hearing under Rule 1102 (S-3, 03:23:26).
  - S-3 (03:23:26) (ruling, Judge Tony Graf Jr.): "reliable hearsay is allowed in a preliminary hearing under the Utah Constitution and under the Rules of Evidence 1102."

### C-12603 (ep 146)
- **Claim:** The host says it is permitted for Lance Twiggs to submit video testimony and that the defense is not able to cross-examine him.

  - S-23 para 59 (tier2_reported, Associated Press (via KSAT)): "Graf indicated he would allow prosecutors to play redacted audio of those statements Thursday."
  - S-40 para 21 (tier2_reported, KSL): "A redacted version of the much-debated video interview with Robinson's roommate, Lance Twiggs, was shown to the courtroom on Thursday morning on the fourth day of Robinson's preliminary hearing."
  - S-41 para 24 (tier2_attributed, defense): "Robinson's defense team, however, says that it prevents them from cross-examining Twiggs, whom they describe as "central" to the state's case."

### C-12606 (ep 146)
- **Claim:** The host says the state is seeking seven charges against Tyler Robinson, the most severe being aggravated murder, which carries a death sentence.
- **Record:** Robinson is charged with capital aggravated murder among other counts and the State seeks death (S-7, 01:42:40; 07:41:21); aggravated murder can carry death (S-10, p. 1).
  - S-7 (01:42:40) (ruling, Judge Tony Graf Jr.): "The defendant is charged with aggravated murder, a capital offense for which the state of Utah is seeking the death penalty"
  - S-7 (07:41:21) (ruling, Judge Tony Graf Jr.): "count one, aggravated murder, a capital offense, together with the state's victim targeting notice. Count two, felony discharge of a firearm causing serious bodily injury, a first degree felony, counts three and four, obstruction of justice, second degree felonies"
  - S-10 p. 1 (official_statement, Utah County Attorney's Office (Jeff Gray)): "aggravated murder carries a penalty of either death, life in prison without the possibility of parole, or twenty-five years to life"

### C-12609 (ep 146)
- **Claim:** The host says the prosecution argues Tyler Robinson is the shooter but also that he chose to fire only one bullet despite having more rounds in the chamber.
- **Record:** The State argued the rifle held four rounds besides the one fired and only one was spent (S-7, 04:10:45; 03:52:13).
  - S-7 (04:10:45) (argument, prosecutor Ryan McBride, Utah County Attorney's Office): "he came loaded with four rounds in that magazine, not just the round that he shot."
  - S-7 (03:52:13) (argument, prosecutor Ryan McBride): "that rifle contained four rounds in it. Only one had been spent."

### C-12610 (ep 146)
- **Claim:** The host says the prosecution alleges text messages where Robinson says he is going to see Charlie Kirk and has to do it because his hate is too much to negotiate, which she says suggests a single target.
- **Record:** The State cited Exhibit 16.1, a text attributed to Robinson: 'I had enough of his hatred. Some hate can't be negotiated out.' (S-7, 06:06:16).
  - S-7 (06:06:16) (exhibit, prosecutor Chad Grunander): "State's Exhibit 16.1, when the defendant texted Mr. Twiggs, "I had enough of his hatred. Some hate can't be negotiated out.""
  - S-7 (04:21:16) (argument, prosecutor Ryan McBride, Utah County Attorney's Office): "Yeah, I had enough of his hatred. Some hate can't be negotiated out."

### C-12615 (ep 146)
- **Claim:** The host says the second charge is felony discharge of a firearm.
- **Record:** Count two is felony discharge of a firearm causing serious bodily injury (S-11, 03:08; S-7, 07:41:21).
  - S-11 (03:08) (charging_allegation, Jeff Gray, Utah County Attorney): "Count two, felony discharge of a firearm causing serious bodily injury, a first degree felony."
  - S-7 (07:41:21) (ruling, Judge Tony Graf Jr.): "count one, aggravated murder, a capital offense, together with the state's victim targeting notice. Count two, felony discharge of a firearm causing serious bodily injury, a first degree felony, counts three and four, obstruction of justice, second degree felonies"

### C-12616 (ep 146)
- **Claim:** The host says the third charge is obstruction of justice because the prosecution alleges Tyler Robinson hid the firearm used to shoot Charlie Kirk.
- **Record:** Count three charges obstruction of justice for moving and concealing the rifle used in the shooting (S-11, 04:36; S-1, p. 2).
  - S-11 (04:36) (charging_allegation, Jeff Gray, Utah County Attorney): "count three, obstruction of justice. A second degree felony for moving and concealing the rifle used in the shooting."
  - S-1 p. 2 (charging_allegation, Utah County Attorney's Office): "(c) concealed or removed the firearm used to shoot Charlie Kirk"

### C-12617 (ep 146)
- **Claim:** The host says the fourth charge is obstruction of justice for allegedly hiding the clothing worn during the shooting, though she notes he is said to have worn the maroon shirt earlier that day.
- **Record:** Count four charges obstruction of justice for disposing of the clothing worn during the shooting (S-11, 04:36), bound over at S-7, 08:26:10.
  - S-11 (04:36) (charging_allegation, Jeff Gray, Utah County Attorney): "Count four, obstruction of justice, a second degree felony for disposing the clothing he wore during the shooting."
  - S-7 (08:26:10) (ruling, Judge Tony Graf Jr.): "defendant concealed or destroyed the clothing with the intent required by the statute. Count four is bound over for trial."

### C-12618 (ep 146)
- **Claim:** The host says the fifth charge is witness tampering for allegedly texting Lance and telling him to delete an exchange.
- **Record:** At the charging press conference (S-11 04:36) Count five is witness tampering for directing the roommate to delete incriminating texts, and the bindover ruling (S-7 08:27:07) notes the text "Delete this exchange."
  - S-11 (04:36) (charging_allegation, Jeff Gray, Utah County Attorney): "Count five, witness tampering, a third degree felony for directing his roommate to delete his incriminating texts."
  - S-7 (08:27:07) (ruling, Judge Tony Graf Jr.): "At the end of this text exchange, defendant texted Twiggs, "Delete this exchange.""

### C-12619 (ep 146)
- **Claim:** The host says the sixth charge is also witness tampering for allegedly texting Lance and telling him not to speak to police and to lawyer up.
- **Record:** S-11 (04:36) charges Count six as witness tampering for directing the roommate to stay silent if police questioned him, based on the text "ask for a lawyer and stay silent" (S-5 exhibit 03:17:54).
  - S-11 (04:36) (charging_allegation, Jeff Gray, Utah County Attorney): "Count six, witness tampering, a third degree felony for directing his roommate to stay silent if police questioned him."
  - S-5 (03:17:54) (exhibit, Agent Davis, SBI (witness)): ""If any police ask you questions, ask for a lawyer and stay silent. My lawyer should be Doug Terry."

### C-12621 (ep 146)
- **Claim:** The host says the seventh charge is a violent offense in the presence of a child, and the defense argues the presumption on a college campus is that no minor children were present.
- **Record:** Count seven is commission of a violent offense in the presence of a child (S-11 04:36; S-7 ruling 07:41:21); the record does not address the host's campus-presumption point.
  - S-11 (04:36) (charging_allegation, Jeff Gray, Utah County Attorney): "count seven, commission of a violent offense in the presence of a child, a class A misdemeanor."
  - S-7 (07:41:21) (ruling, Judge Tony Graf Jr.): "counts five and six, tampering with a witness, third degree felonies, and count seven, a violent offense committed in the presence of a child, a class B misdemeanor, which the state alleges is reclassified as a class A misdemeanor"

### C-12642 (ep 147)
- **Claim:** The host says Judge Graf ruled that the state of Utah met its reliable-hearsay probable-cause standard to bind the case over to trial.
- **Record:** Judge Graf ruled the State demonstrated probable cause on the identity and homicide elements of Count one (S-7 08:10:47) in the bindover ruling.
  - S-7 (08:10:47) (ruling, Judge Tony Graf Jr.): "Accordingly, the State has demonstrated probable cause as to the identity and homicide elements of count one."

### C-12645 (ep 147)
- **Claim:** The host says Judge Graf declined to dismiss Count 6 (witness tampering for the "ask for a lawyer and stay silent" text to Lance Twiggs) over the defense's constitutional-rights argument, and she plays a clip of the ruling.
- **Record:** The ruling cites the September 11, 2025 "ask for a lawyer and stay silent" text and binds Count 6 over for trial (S-7 08:28:42, 08:30:46).
  - S-7 (08:28:42) (ruling, Judge Tony Graf Jr.): "The evidence shows that on September 11th, 2025, defendant sent a text message to Lance Twiggs stating, "If any police ask you questions, ask for a lawyer and stay silent.""
  - S-7 (08:30:46) (ruling, Judge Tony Graf Jr.): "Count 6 is bound over for trial."

### C-12646 (ep 147)
- **Claim:** The host says Count 5 is also witness tampering but pertains strictly to the message in which Tyler Robinson allegedly told Lance Twiggs to "delete this exchange," a separate charge from Count 6.
- **Record:** Count five is witness tampering for directing the roommate to delete texts (S-11 04:36), tied to the "Delete this exchange" text and bound over separately (S-7 08:27:07-08:27:40).
  - S-11 (04:36) (charging_allegation, Jeff Gray, Utah County Attorney): "Count five, witness tampering, a third degree felony for directing his roommate to delete his incriminating texts."
  - S-7 (08:27:07) (ruling, Judge Tony Graf Jr.): "At the end of this text exchange, defendant texted Twiggs, "Delete this exchange.""
  - S-7 (08:27:40) (ruling, Judge Tony Graf Jr.): "Count five is bound over for trial."

### C-12647 (ep 147)
- **Claim:** The host says the court permitted Count 6 to proceed on the basis of Count 5, and in a clip she plays of the ruling the court says the surrounding communications, including the alleged "delete this exchange" instruction, permit a reasonable inference that the defendant intended Twiggs to withhold information from investigators.
- **Record:** The ruling says the communications viewed together permit a reasonable inference Robinson intended Twiggs to withhold information from investigators, and Count 6 was bound over (S-7 08:30:00-08:30:46).
  - S-7 (08:30:00) (ruling, Judge Tony Graf Jr.): "Viewed together in the light most favorable to the State, those communications permit a reasonable inference that Defendant intended to cause Twiggs to withhold information from investigators."
  - S-7 (08:30:46) (ruling, Judge Tony Graf Jr.): "Count 6 is bound over for trial."

### C-12648 (ep 147)
- **Claim:** In a clip of the ruling the host plays, the court says that, standing alone, the text "ask for a lawyer and stay silent" describes lawful advice concerning constitutional rights and does not establish witness tampering.
- **Record:** Judge Graf ruled that standing alone, advising someone to request counsel and remain silent is lawful and does not establish witness tampering (S-7 08:29:04).
  - S-7 (08:29:04) (ruling, Judge Tony Graf Jr.): "Standing alone, advising another person to request counsel and remain silent is lawful and does not establish witness tampering."

### C-12649 (ep 147)
- **Claim:** In an arraignment clip the host plays, on September 1, 2026, defense counsel enters pleas of not guilty on Tyler Robinson's behalf to all counts in the information.

  - S-7 (08:43:00) (argument, defense attorney Kathryn Nester): "waive reading of the information and enter pleas of not guilty on Mr. Robinson's behalf to all counts."
  - S-7 (08:43:08) (ruling, Judge Tony Graf Jr.): "The Court finds that that is knowingly and voluntarily made"

### C-12650 (ep 147)
- **Claim:** In an arraignment clip the host plays, defense counsel says he waives reading of the information before entering not-guilty pleas.
- **Record:** Defense counsel waived reading of the information before entering not-guilty pleas (S-7 08:43:00); the counsel was Kathryn Nester.
  - S-7 (08:43:00) (argument, defense attorney Kathryn Nester): "waive reading of the information and enter pleas of not guilty on Mr. Robinson's behalf to all counts."

### C-12662 (ep 147)
- **Claim:** The host says that, based on the security team's testimonies, the two security vehicles (a black Jeep Wagoneer and a Lincoln Navigator) arrived at UVU between 9:00 and 9:30 a.m., independently of Charlie Kirk, while he was at the restaurantology event in Salt Lake City.
