  - S-12 p. 43 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report Project Team): "On the morning of September 10, the TPUSA security team arrived at UVU with Dan Flood at approximately 9:00 a.m."

### C-12689 (ep 147)
- **Claim:** In testimony played on air, Blake Neff says he and Mikey leaving was not abandoning Charlie because Charlie had a security team that left immediately into action and got Charlie out.
- **Record:** The After-Action Report says the TPUSA security team evacuated Charlie Kirk from the area immediately (S-12 p. 49).
  - S-12 p. 49 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report Project Team): "helping to ensure TPUSA security team was able to evacuate Charlie Kirk from the area immediately"

### C-12706 (ep 147)
- **Claim:** Novak says in the audio played that the evidence from Mr. Twiggs is that he identified in a non-gender-conforming way to his friends and was in a romantic relationship with Mr. Robinson.
- **Record:** Novak argued at closing that the evidence from Twiggs is that he identified in a non-gender-conforming way and was in a romantic relationship with Robinson (S-7 05:19:06).
  - S-7 (05:19:06) (argument, defense attorney Mr. Novak): "it is true that the evidence from Mr. Twiggs is that he identified in a non-gender conforming way to his friends and that he was in a romantic relationship with Mr. Robinson."

### C-12711 (ep 147)
- **Claim:** The host says the state is pursuing sentence enhancements on the theory that Charlie Kirk was targeted for his political or religious beliefs, with the "some hate just can't be negotiated" text as the central anchor.
- **Record:** The State alleges Robinson targeted Kirk for his political expression (S-11 03:08), and the ruling cites the "Some hate can't be negotiated out" text on motive (S-7 08:37:48); 'central anchor' is the host's characterisation.
  - S-11 (03:08) (charging_allegation, Jeff Gray, Utah County Attorney): "the defendant is believed to have targeted Charlie Kirk based on Charlie Kirk's political expression and did so knowing that children were present and would witness the homicide."
  - S-7 (08:37:48) (ruling, Judge Tony Graf Jr.): "When asked why he shot Mr. Kirk, Defendant allegedly responded, "I had enough of his hatred. Some hate can't be negotiated out.""

### C-12810 (ep 148)
- **Claim:** The host says Rabbi Wolicki was on the call, correcting herself that it was a Zoom call rather than FaceTime because he was in Arizona, and that Josh Hammer was on it too.
- **Record:** Fox News Digital (S-15) reports Rabbi Pesach Wolicki spoke with Kirk via Zoom the night before the assassination; Josh Hammer's presence and Wolicki being in Arizona are not covered.
  - S-15 para 162 (tier2_attributed, Pesach Wolicki): "Rabbi Pesach Wolicki, who spoke with Charlie Kirk via Zoom the night before his assassination, told Fox News Digital"

### C-12815 (ep 148)
- **Claim:** The host says that when Erika gave interviews after the shooting she said Charlie was just excited the night before, which the host finds odd next to his fear of dying.
- **Record:** The New York Times (S-26) reports Erika Kirk saying Charlie Kirk was too excited to sleep on his last night; the host's view that this is odd is her own.
  - S-26 para 33 (tier2_attributed, Erika Kirk): "on what turned out to be the last night of Charlie Kirk, her husband, he was too excited to sleep."

### C-12901 (ep 149)
- **Claim:** Fox News published a piece characterizing the alleged Cowboy Smokehouse meal as "one of Tyler Robinson's last meals as a free man…a steak dinner, medium rare."
- **Record:** Fox News (S-65) opens 'One of Tyler Robinson's last meals as a free man may have been at a roadside steakhouse' and quotes the owner saying he had a sirloin, medium rare.
  - S-65 para 156 (tier2_attributed, unnamed Panguitch restaurant owner): "One of Tyler Robinson 's last meals as a free man may have been at a roadside steakhouse off the beaten path, according to a Utah restaurateur who called in a tip to the FBI"
  - S-65 para 162 (tier2_attributed, unnamed Panguitch restaurant owner): ""He had a steak — had a sirloin, medium rare," he said. "Vegetables. Baked potato.""

### C-12909 (ep 149)
- **Claim:** Per the host's account, the server contacted the owner after believing he had served the suspect; the owner then contacted the FBI.
- **Record:** Fox News (S-65) quotes the Panguitch restaurant owner saying a server was relatively certain she had served the man pictured and that this was turned over to the FBI, and describes him as having called in a tip to the FBI.
  - S-65 para 168 (tier2_attributed, unnamed Panguitch restaurant owner): "I had a server say that they were relatively certain that they had had that person in that night — late,"
  - S-65 para 168 (tier2_attributed, unnamed Panguitch restaurant owner): "That was turned over to the FBI ."
  - S-65 para 156 (tier2_attributed, unnamed Panguitch restaurant owner): "One of Tyler Robinson 's last meals as a free man may have been at a roadside steakhouse off the beaten path, according to a Utah restaurateur who called in a tip to the FBI"

### C-12986 (ep 150)
- **Claim:** Host reports that Donald Trump tweeted a mock-up of a Time magazine cover depicting her as "the most vile person of the year."
- **Record:** The New York Times (S-66) reports Trump shared a doctored image calling Owens 'vile person of the year'; the post was on Truth Social, not a tweet, and the Time-cover format is not described.
  - S-66 para 25 (tier2_attributed, Donald Trump): "Trump got involved, posting on Truth Social that Owens is an “extremely Low IQ individual” and sharing a doctored image that referred to Owens as “vile person of the year.”"

### C-13099 (ep 152)
- **Claim:** The host says Bagley's body cam footage notoriously went off after about 25 minutes.
- **Record:** Bagley confirmed his only body-cam recording ran 27 minutes 35 seconds and ended on the roof, he thinks because the battery died (S-2 04:12:23-04:12:54).
  - S-2 (04:12:40) (exhibit, defense attorney Kathryn Nester): "So that 27 minute and 35 second body cam that starts when you're at the Hall of Flags and ends while you're still on the roof but haven't put the crime tape up yet"
  - S-2 (04:12:54) (testimony, Officer Bagley, UVU Police (witness)): "Yes."
  - S-2 (04:12:23) (testimony, Officer Bagley, UVU Police (witness)): "I think the battery went dead. It was just right at that moment."

### C-13181 (ep 153)
- **Claim:** The host says Charlie Kirk's family has filed a notice of claim against the State of Utah, the UVU Police Department, UVU Police Chief Jeffrey Long, former UVU president Astrid Tuminez, and others presently "unknown" involved in Charlie Kirk's death.
- **Record:** KSL (S-60) reports the Kirk family's notice of claim names UVU, the UVU Police Department, Chief Jeffrey Long, former president Astrid Tuminez and the state of Utah; the 'unknown' parties are not mentioned.
  - S-60 para 18 (tier2_reported, KSL): "against UVU, the UVU Police Department, police Chief Jeffrey Long, former UVU President Astrid Tuminez and the state of Utah."
  - S-60 para 17 (tier2_reported, KSL): "Charlie Kirk's family has put Utah Valley University and the state of Utah on notice that it intends to sue."

### C-13186 (ep 153)
- **Claim:** Per an Associated Press article the host shows, published September 22, 2025, Utah Valley University announced on Monday an independent external review of the fatal shooting of Charlie Kirk on its campus; the host remarks 'just 12 days after Charlie is hit.'
- **Record:** The After-Action Report says UVU requested an external, independent review and retained two outside firms (S-12 p. 5, p. 112); the AP date is not addressed.
  - S-12 p. 5 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report (Lake Forest Group / Brownstein Hyatt Farber Schreck)): "This external and independent assessment was done at the request of Utah Valley University (UVU)."
  - S-12 p. 112 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report independent review team): "Retained two outside firms to conduct an independent review and After-Action Report concerning the events surrounding September 10, 2025"

### C-13203 (ep 153)
- **Claim:** The host reads the first negligence point: UVU police did not conduct a meaningful risk assessment and UVU did not hold a security briefing at which rooftop risks, already communicated to Chief Long, could be passed to other officers.
- **Record:** KSL (S-60) reports the notice of claim says UVU and its police failed to conduct a meaningful risk assessment and that rooftop concerns had been passed to UVU police; the security-briefing detail is not covered.
  - S-60 para 25 (tier2_attributed, notice of claim (Kirk family)): "The notice states that Utah Valley University and its police department failed to conduct a meaningful risk assessment"
  - S-60 para 28 (tier2_attributed, notice of claim (Kirk family)): "Turning Point USA staff members had even passed along concerns from students to UVU police about the risk rooftop access posed to Kirk."

### C-13204 (ep 153)
- **Claim:** The host says she is told that on August 25, 2025, TPUSA events committee employee Maycee Crofts insisted during a Microsoft Teams call that the event be held in the amphitheater, and that UVU acquiesced; she says this is not in the filing.
- **Record:** The After-Action Report says Maycee Crofts joined the August 25 MEAC Teams meeting and UVU agreed to TPUSA's outdoor venue when she insisted on it (S-12 p. 40, p. 48).
  - S-12 p. 40 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report Project Team): "During the August 25 MEAC Teams meeting, Maycee Crofts,22 a Senior Field Representative for TPUSA, participated at the request of TPUSA."
  - S-12 p. 48 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report Project Team): "multiple interviews the Project Team conducted indicated that UVU had expressed concerns about the outdoor location, including during the August 25 MEAC meeting, but ultimately agreed to TPUSA’s requested venue location when TPUSA representative Maycee Crofts insisted on it."

### C-13205 (ep 153)
- **Claim:** The host says she is told that on the August 25 Teams call UVU said the event should not be in the amphitheater because there is no traffic control and that the university could not shut the amphitheater down, and that this is not in the filing.
- **Record:** The UVU After-Action Report finds UVU expressed concerns about the outdoor location, including during the August 25 MEAC meeting, before agreeing when TPUSA's Maycee Crofts insisted (S-12 p. 48); the stated reasons (traffic control, inability to shut it down) are not addressed.
  - S-12 p. 48 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report Project Team): "multiple interviews the Project Team conducted indicated that UVU had expressed concerns about the outdoor location, including during the August 25 MEAC meeting, but ultimately agreed to TPUSA’s requested venue location when TPUSA representative Maycee Crofts insisted on it."

### C-13210 (ep 153)
- **Claim:** The host says members of Charlie's security team first arrived on the UVU campus at 9:00 AM on the morning of September 10, 2025.
- **Record:** The After-Action Report says the TPUSA security team arrived on campus with Dan Flood at about 9:00 a.m. on September 10 (S-12 p. 20, p. 43).
  - S-12 p. 43 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report Project Team): "On the morning of September 10, the TPUSA security team arrived at UVU with Dan Flood at approximately 9:00 a.m."
  - S-12 p. 20 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report (Lake Forest Group / Brownstein Hyatt Farber Schreck)): "~9:00 a.m. TPUSA security team arrived on campus with Dan Flood."

### C-13212 (ep 153)
- **Claim:** The notice of claim alleges UVU assigned only six campus police officers to the outdoor tour event that ultimately attracted approximately 3,000 attendees.
- **Record:** Officer Bagley testified six UVU officers were at the event (S-2 02:44:36); the attendance figure is not addressed.
  - S-2 (02:44:36) (testimony, Officer Bagley, UVU Police (witness)): "There was six of us that were, there was patrol one and then five others that were there with the chief."

### C-13215 (ep 153)
- **Claim:** The notice of claim alleges that the request for additional officers was denied.
- **Record:** KSL (S-60) reports the notice of claim says only six officers were assigned and Tuminez denied a request to add more.
  - S-60 para 25 (tier2_attributed, notice of claim (Kirk family)): "Only six police officers were assigned to work the event, Tuminez denied a request to add more officers"

### C-13227 (ep 153)
- **Claim:** The host says Turning Point USA was the party that controlled the tickets, rebutting the notice's claim that UVU could have restricted attendee numbers.
- **Record:** The After-Action Report says TPUSA managed ticketing through its national office (S-12 p. 66).
  - S-12 p. 66 (report_finding, Utah Valley University After-Action Report (Project Team)): "TPUSA managed ticketing through its national office and did not share anticipated attendee numbers"

### C-13229 (ep 153)
- **Claim:** Per the text message the host shows, Dan Flood told Chief Jeffrey Long that a student group said there is student roof access pretty close to where CK will be set up and that the Sorenson Student Center has a couple of staircases that go up to the walkways on the roof.
- **Record:** Dan Flood's September 8 text to Chief Long says there is student roof access close to where CK will be set up via Sorensen Student Center staircases (S-12 p. 43).
  - S-12 p. 43 (exhibit, Dan Flood, TPUSA Security Supervisor (text to Chief Long, Sept 8, 2025)): "there is student roof access pretty close to where CK will be set up at Utah Valley (The Sorensen Student center has a couple stair cases that go up to the walkways on the roof)"

### C-13230 (ep 153)
- **Claim:** The host says the thread asks for either controlled access to the roof walkways or permission for one of TPUSA's security to be there, and describes it as an either-or request.
- **Record:** Flood's text asks to either have it controlled access or allow one of my guys to be there (S-12 p. 43).
  - S-12 p. 43 (exhibit, Dan Flood, TPUSA Security Supervisor (text to Chief Long, Sept 8, 2025)): "I was told students have access above us, if this is true, it would be nice to either have it controlled access or allow one of my guys to be there as well."

### C-13231 (ep 153)
- **Claim:** The host says her outlet reported in November, after Harpole's Shawn Ryan appearance, that the thread referred to the walkway above Charlie's head, reachable by a staircase from the lower level of Sorenson.
- **Record:** The After-Action Report finds Dan Flood's September 8 text to Chief Long concerned the Hall of Flags walkway above the canopy where Charlie Kirk would speak (S-12 p. 43, p. 19).
  - S-12 p. 43 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report Project Team): "On September 8, at approximately 3:12 p.m., Dan Flood sent Chief Long a text message regarding the Hall of Flags outdoor pedestrian walkway located above the canopy area where Charlie Kirk would be speaking."
  - S-12 p. 19 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report (Lake Forest Group / Brownstein Hyatt Farber Schreck)): "TPUSA security raised a rooftop/outdoor pedestrian walkway access concern regarding the area above where Charlie Kirk’s canopy was to be placed."

### C-13234 (ep 153)
- **Claim:** The host says that with the omitted line restored the text clearly concerns a specific area right above Charlie's team and has nothing to do with the Losee Center or any other rooftop.
- **Record:** The After-Action Report says TPUSA's concern was the area directly above the speaker location (S-12 p. 67), and Flood's text reads 'Concerns of agitators above us' (S-12 p. 44).
  - S-12 p. 67 (report_finding, Utah Valley University After-Action Report (Project Team)): "This measure helped restrict access to the area directly above the speaker location after TPUSA raised concerns about that location."
  - S-12 p. 44 (exhibit, Dan Flood, TPUSA Security Supervisor (text to Chief Long, Sept 10, 2025)): "Concerns of agitators above us."

### C-13235 (ep 153)
- **Claim:** Per the text message the host shows, Chief Jeffrey Long responded to Dan Flood's rooftop-access request with the message "I got you covered."
- **Record:** The text exhibit shows Chief Long replied to Flood "I got you covered" (S-12 p. 43).
  - S-12 p. 43 (exhibit, Chief Long (text to Dan Flood, Sept 8, 2025)): "Chief Long: I got you covered"

### C-13294 (ep 154)
- **Claim:** The host says, reading the ABC report, that the TPUSA chapter at UVU filled out the form to host the event in mid July, on July 18.
- **Record:** The After-Action Report timeline says the UVU TPUSA Club submitted its formal event request on July 18 (S-12 p. 18).
  - S-12 p. 18 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report (Lake Forest Group / Brownstein Hyatt Farber Schreck)): "July 18 Formal event request submitted by UVU TPUSA Club. Event described as an appearance by Charlie Kirk to discuss current events. External ticketing by TPUSA anticipated."

### C-13298 (ep 154)
- **Claim:** The host reads that Chief Jeff Long told reporters there were probably over 3,000 people in attendance, that six officers worked the event and that Kirk had his own security team.
- **Record:** Bagley testified six UVU officers worked the event and that Kirk had his own security team present (S-2 02:44:36, 02:46:51); the attendance figure is not addressed.
  - S-2 (02:44:36) (testimony, Officer Bagley, UVU Police (witness)): "There was six of us that were, there was patrol one and then five others that were there with the chief."
  - S-2 (02:46:51) (testimony, Officer Bagley, UVU Police (witness)): "Yes."

### C-13302 (ep 154)
- **Claim:** The host reads that an unnamed person with a UVU email address wrote on August 21 suggesting the group would be better placed at the Fulton Library Quad if it drew a large crowd, while the event stayed in the Student Center courtyard.
- **Record:** The After-Action Report says UVU officials suggested alternatives such as the Fulton Library Quad (S-12 p. 41); the August 21 date is not addressed.
  - S-12 p. 41 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report Project Team): "UVU officials suggested alternative locations such as the Fulton Library Quad or interior locations where access could be more effectively controlled."

### C-13303 (ep 154)
- **Claim:** The host reads that a TPUSA official told ABC News two UVU emergency medical personnel were on site but not an ambulance.
- **Record:** The After-Action Report says two UVU EMTs were assigned and no ambulance was staged on site (S-12 p. 59).
  - S-12 p. 59 (report_finding, Utah Valley University After-Action Report (Project Team)): "the Director of Emergency Management and Safety assigned two UVU Emergency Medical Services (EMS) personnel. Both were Emergency Medical Technicians (EMTs)"
  - S-12 p. 59 (report_finding, Utah Valley University After-Action Report (Project Team)): "No ambulance was staged on site for the September 10 TPUSA event."

### C-13382 (ep 155)
- **Claim:** The host says neighbor Miss Noble and her husband saw a Dodge Challenger parked in their neighborhood shortly after Officer Goforth's sighting, and that Miss Noble claims it was being driven by a bald person with two people in the back seat.
- **Record:** Agent Hull testified the vehicle came to the Noble residence a short time after the Goforth interaction and that the Noble report described the driver as bald (S-2 06:59:53; S-3 02:06:53); back-seat passengers are not addressed.
  - S-2 (06:59:53) (testimony, SBI Agent David Hull (witness)): "And a short period after that, the vehicle comes to that house"
  - S-3 (02:06:53) (testimony, Agent Hull, former Utah State Bureau of Investigation agent (witness; Rev also renders Holt/Hall)): "I do remember that being in the report, yes."

### C-13402 (ep 155)
- **Claim:** The host reads the September 15 warrant narrative as saying the video shows Robinson wearing a ball cap, a red t-shirt, light colored shorts and Converse style tennis shoes.
- **Record:** Agent Hull testified the video shows the driver in a red/maroon T-shirt, shorts and Converse shoes (S-3 00:45:39, 00:47:48).
  - S-3 (00:45:39) (testimony, SBI Agent Hull (witness)): "The driver of the vehicle exits the vehicle and then walks on foot to exit the parking garage. It's the individual in the red T-shirt and the shorts and the shoes."
  - S-3 (00:47:48) (testimony, SBI Agent Hull (witness)): "It's a maroon color T-shirt with gray or olive colored shorts and then converse shoes."

### C-13403 (ep 155)
- **Claim:** The host says Hull's preliminary hearing account has Maroon Boy return on foot at 10:09 a.m., go to Chick-fil-A, sit down and eat, and then walk around campus to the wooded area where the gun was eventually found.
- **Record:** The ruling and Hull's testimony say Robinson returned on foot just after 10:00, ate at Chick-fil-A in the Sorenson Center, then walked to the wooded area (S-7 07:49:47; S-3 00:48:15).
  - S-7 (07:49:47) (ruling, Judge Tony Graf Jr.): "Video surveillance captured a person identified as Mr. Robinson returning to campus on foot from the northeast just after 10:00 AM. He ate at Chick-fil-A in the Sorenson Center, one of the buildings adjacent to the outdoor amphitheater."
  - S-7 (07:49:47) (ruling, Judge Tony Graf Jr.): "He then walked to the northeast of campus to a wooded area and on the north side of campus drive."
  - S-3 (00:48:15) (testimony, SBI Agent Hull (witness)): "He went to Chick-fil-A in the Sorenson building and purchased some food, sat and ate the food."

### C-13513 (ep 156)
- **Claim:** The host says, replying to a viewer comment about license plates, that in Officer Goforth's testimony they kept saying a partial plate was run and matched, which she finds unusual.
- **Record:** Agent Hull testified Officer Goforth ran a partial plate (S-3 00:59:18); the host's view that this is unusual is opinion.
  - S-3 (00:59:18) (testimony, SBI Agent Hull (witness)): "Yes, he ran a partial plate."

### C-13514 (ep 156)
- **Claim:** The host says, answering a viewer comment about license plates, that her team has noticed officials repeatedly say they confirmed the first part of the plate, got a partial plate and ran it, including in Officer Goforth's testimony.
- **Record:** Agent Hull testified Goforth noted the plate and ran a partial plate (S-3 00:58:38, 00:59:18).
  - S-3 (00:58:38) (testimony, SBI Agent Hull (witness)): "there was something about the interaction that made him make a note of the license plate of the vehicle"
  - S-3 (00:59:18) (testimony, SBI Agent Hull (witness)): "Yes, he ran a partial plate."

### C-13520 (ep 157)
- **Claim:** The host says UVU almost immediately engaged a third party, the Lake Forest Group, to conduct a broad review of the security protocols for September 10, opening the campus and police to interviews.
- **Record:** The After-Action Report says the Lake Forest Group supported UVU's review of the September 10 event and conducted over 70 interviews, including UVU police (S-12 p. 6, p. 8).
  - S-12 p. 6 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report (Lake Forest Group / Brownstein Hyatt Farber Schreck)): "The Lake Forest Group (LFG) assembled a highly qualified core team to support Utah Valley University (UVU) in this important review of the September 10, 2025 TPUSA event held at UVU."
  - S-12 p. 8 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report (Lake Forest Group / Brownstein Hyatt Farber Schreck)): "Conducted over 70 interviews with University leadership, faculty, staff, students, UVU Police Department personnel, and external public safety partners"

### C-13522 (ep 157)
- **Claim:** The Lake Forest Group independent review documents that despite repeated outreach (phone, voicemail, certified letter, and Phoenix senior-staff contact), Brian Harpole, Dan Flood, Maycee Crofts, and the UVU TPUSA club president did not participate, while UVU and public agencies did cooperate.
- **Record:** The After-Action Report says Flood, Crofts and the UVU TPUSA club head did not respond to calls, voicemails, certified letters or outreach via TPUSA senior staff in Phoenix (S-12 p. 8, p. 42).
  - S-12 p. 8 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report (Lake Forest Group / Brownstein Hyatt Farber Schreck)): "Neither Flood nor Crofts responded to phone calls or voicemail messages."
  - S-12 p. 8 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report (Lake Forest Group / Brownstein Hyatt Farber Schreck)): "The Project Team further contacted senior-level staff members at TPUSA in Phoenix seeking assistance in connecting with Dan Flood and Maycee Crofts. The Project Team did not receive a response"
  - S-12 p. 8 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report (Lake Forest Group / Brownstein Hyatt Farber Schreck)): "He did not respond to the Project Team’s various outreach efforts, which included a certified letter seeking his cooperation."
  - S-12 p. 42 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report Project Team): "The Project Team also sent Flood a certified letter requesting an interview. Flood did not respond to these outreach efforts."

### C-13523 (ep 157)
- **Claim:** The host reads that Dan Flood was identified as the person who coordinated security for the organization with Brian Harpole of Integrity Solutions, was present at UVU on September 10, and had phone and text communication with UVU police chief Jeff Long a few days before and the morning of the event.
- **Record:** The After-Action Report says Flood coordinated security with Brian Harpole of Integrity Solutions, was at UVU on September 10 and had phone and text contact with Chief Long (S-12 p. 7).
  - S-12 p. 7 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report (Lake Forest Group / Brownstein Hyatt Farber Schreck)): "Flood was identified as the person who coordinated security for the organization with Brian Harpole of Integrity Solutions."
  - S-12 p. 7 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report (Lake Forest Group / Brownstein Hyatt Farber Schreck)): "Flood was present at UVU on September 10 and had phone and text communications with UVU Police Chief Jeff Long a few days before and the morning of the event."

### C-13524 (ep 157)
- **Claim:** The host reads that Maycee Crofts, a TPUSA senior field representative, participated online in the major events assessment committee planning meeting held on August 25 for the September 10 TPUSA event.
- **Record:** The After-Action Report says Senior Field Representative Maycee Crofts joined the August 25 MEAC planning meeting online (S-12 p. 8, p. 40).
  - S-12 p. 8 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report (Lake Forest Group / Brownstein Hyatt Farber Schreck)): "participated online in the Major Events Assessment Committee (MEAC) planning meeting held on August 25 regarding the September 10 TPUSA event."
  - S-12 p. 40 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report Project Team): "During the August 25 MEAC Teams meeting, Maycee Crofts,22 a Senior Field Representative for TPUSA, participated at the request of TPUSA."

### C-13525 (ep 157)
- **Claim:** The host reads that a Utah risk-management official communicated with a TPUSA executive and urged the named TPUSA-associated individuals to cooperate with the assessment, and there was no response.
- **Record:** The After-Action Report says a Utah risk-management official urged a TPUSA executive to have the named individuals cooperate and there was no response (S-12 p. 8).
  - S-12 p. 8 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report (Lake Forest Group / Brownstein Hyatt Farber Schreck)): "A Utah risk management official had communications with an executive of TPUSA and urged that the above-named individuals who were associated with TPUSA on September 10, 2025 cooperate with this assessment by being interviewed by the Project Team. There was no response."

### C-13526 (ep 157)
- **Claim:** The host reads the review's stated opinion that at no time before or after the assassination did any employee or agent of UVU act in bad faith or in willful and wanton disregard for the safety of others, while identifying actions and practices that warrant attention.
- **Record:** The After-Action Report gives the Project Team's opinion that no UVU employee or agent acted in bad faith or in willful and wanton disregard for others' safety (S-12 p. 11).
  - S-12 p. 11 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report (Lake Forest Group / Brownstein Hyatt Farber Schreck)): "It is the opinion of the Project Team, based on our review of all available information, that at no time during the events leading up to the assassination of Charlie Kirk, or in its aftermath, did any employee or agent of UVU act in bad faith"
  - S-12 p. 11 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report (Lake Forest Group / Brownstein Hyatt Farber Schreck)): "or in willful and wanton disregard for the safety of others."

### C-13527 (ep 157)
- **Claim:** The host reads the review's finding that the MEAC process began on August 21 but several key event security decisions were deferred to TPUSA's field representative even though UVU staff raised concerns about the outdoor fountain courtyard and TPUSA ticketing.
- **Record:** The After-Action Report says MEAC began August 21 and key security decisions, including the Fountain Courtyard and ticketing, were deferred to the TPUSA field representative despite staff concerns (S-12 p. 12, p. 40).
  - S-12 p. 12 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report (Lake Forest Group / Brownstein Hyatt Farber Schreck)): "the MEAC process was initiated on August 21 to determine whether the event should proceed and to identify security concerns."
  - S-12 p. 12 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report (Lake Forest Group / Brownstein Hyatt Farber Schreck)): "several key event-security decisions were deferred to the TPUSA field representative even though UVU staff members raised concerns"
  - S-12 p. 40 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report Project Team): "reflected considerable deference on UVU’s part to TPUSA’s expressed desires regarding event logistics, including the Fountain Courtyard as the venue and the ticketing process."

### C-13528 (ep 157)
- **Claim:** The host reads the review's finding that holding the event in the fountain courtyard and using TPUSA's own ticketing placed the event in an area more difficult to control and limited UVU's ability to obtain reliable real-time attendance information.
- **Record:** The After-Action Report says those decisions placed the event in an area harder to control and limited UVU's real-time attendance information (S-12 p. 12).
  - S-12 p. 12 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report (Lake Forest Group / Brownstein Hyatt Farber Schreck)): "Those decisions placed the event in an area that was more difficult to control and limited UVU’s ability to obtain reliable, real-time information regarding anticipated attendance and crowd size."

### C-13529 (ep 157)
- **Claim:** The host reads the review's statement that, because the event was on UVU's campus, UVU should have retained decision-making authority over key security issues including venue selection, ticketing and access control.
- **Record:** The After-Action Report says site-selection authority should have been with the University and UVU should have insisted on choosing the venue and using its own ticketing (S-12 p. 54, p. 71).
  - S-12 p. 54 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report Project Team): "TPUSA was the final decision maker for the event site selection, but this authority should be with the University."
  - S-12 p. 71 (report_finding, Utah Valley University After-Action Report (Project Team)): "It also might well have highlighted the need to insist that UVU determine the venue location due to security concerns, as well as insist that the UVU ticketing system be used instead of TPUSA’s"

### C-13532 (ep 157)
- **Claim:** The independent-review timeline states that on July 10, 2025, at the request of TPUSA's field representative (whom the host identifies as Maycee Crofts), the UVU club president was asked to request that Charlie Kirk speak in the courtyard, characterized by the host as a reversal of normal TPUSA chapter event-request practice.
- **Record:** On July 10, 2025, at the TPUSA field representative's request, the UVU club president asked the Clubs Office about using the Courtyard on September 10 (S-12 p. 18, p. 41); 'reversal of practice' is the host's characterisation.
  - S-12 p. 18 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report (Lake Forest Group / Brownstein Hyatt Farber Schreck)): "At the request of the TPUSA field representative, UVU TPUSA Club President contacted the Clubs Office requesting the specific use of the Fountain Courtyard for a September 10 TPUSA event."
  - S-12 p. 41 (exhibit, TPUSA UVU Club President (email to UVU Club Services, July 10, 2025)): "My field representative for Turning Point USA has asked me to reach out to the UVU Club Department to ask about availability on hosting an event on the Courtyard on September 10 from 9am-4pm"

### C-13534 (ep 157)
- **Claim:** The host reads the review's verbatim email in which the UVU TPUSA club president tells UVU club services that his TPUSA field representative asked him to ask about hosting an event in the courtyard on September 10 from 9:00 a.m. to 4:00 p.m., and that she was quite vague on what the event was.
- **Record:** The July 10 email asks about hosting on the Courtyard September 10, 9am-4pm, at the field representative's request and says she was quite vague (S-12 p. 41).
  - S-12 p. 41 (exhibit, TPUSA UVU Club President (email to UVU Club Services, July 10, 2025)): "My field representative for Turning Point USA has asked me to reach out to the UVU Club Department to ask about availability on hosting an event on the Courtyard on September 10 from 9am-4pm"
  - S-12 p. 41 (exhibit, TPUSA UVU Club President (email to UVU Club Services, July 10, 2025)): "Shes [sic] being quite vague on what this event is, so unfortunately this is all the information I have in regards to it."

### C-13535 (ep 157)
- **Claim:** The host reads the review timeline entry that on July 18 the club submitted an event request describing an appearance by Charlie Kirk to discuss current events, with ticketing handled externally by TPUSA and no university oversight.
- **Record:** The timeline says the July 18 formal request described a Charlie Kirk appearance to discuss current events, with external TPUSA ticketing anticipated (S-12 p. 18).
  - S-12 p. 18 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report (Lake Forest Group / Brownstein Hyatt Farber Schreck)): "July 18 Formal event request submitted by UVU TPUSA Club. Event described as an appearance by Charlie Kirk to discuss current events. External ticketing by TPUSA anticipated."

### C-13536 (ep 157)

- **Record:** The timeline says on July 28 the Clubs Office required annual club registration before the event could be considered, and it was submitted July 29 (S-12 p. 18).
  - S-12 p. 18 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report (Lake Forest Group / Brownstein Hyatt Farber Schreck)): "July 28 Clubs Office advised TPUSA Club that annual club registration had to be completed before the event could be considered."
  - S-12 p. 18 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report (Lake Forest Group / Brownstein Hyatt Farber Schreck)): "July 29 TPUSA club registration submitted."

### C-13537 (ep 157)
- **Claim:** The host reads that in response to UVU's questionnaire TPUSA said it expected 600 attendees, that TPUSA National handled registrations, and that TPUSA offered to connect UVUPD with TPUSA security and said there was no risk factor for safety or security.
- **Record:** The After-Action Report says TPUSA projected about 600 attendees, offered to connect UVUPD with its security and reported no risk or safety concerns (S-12 p. 18, p. 42).
  - S-12 p. 18 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report (Lake Forest Group / Brownstein Hyatt Farber Schreck)): "TPUSA projected approximately 600 attendees, offered to connect UVUPD with TPUSA security, and stated that they had no risk factor/safety/security concerns."
  - S-12 p. 42 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report Project Team): "represented no risk factors/ safety/security concerns or need for emergency medical services, and offered to connect UVUPD with TPUSA’s head of security."

### C-13538 (ep 157)
- **Claim:** The host reads that the August 21 email convening the major events committee was the first time UVU police chief Jeff Long became aware of the event.
- **Record:** The After-Action Report says the August 21 Event Services email convening MEAC appears to be when Chief Long first learned of the event (S-12 p. 18, p. 40).
  - S-12 p. 18 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report (Lake Forest Group / Brownstein Hyatt Farber Schreck)): "This is when UVUPD Chief Long first became aware of the September 10 TPUSA event."
  - S-12 p. 40 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report Project Team): "On August 21, 2025, Event Services sent an email to those required to attend the MEAC meeting scheduled for August 25, 2025. This appears to have been the point at which UVU Police Chief Long first became aware of the event."

  - S-12 p. 18 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report (Lake Forest Group / Brownstein Hyatt Farber Schreck)): "UVU Free Speech Committee flagged the event because of anticipated protests and reviewed insights from prior TPUSA appearances, including an appearance of Charlie Kirk at UVU in 2019."

### C-13541 (ep 157)

  - S-12 p. 40 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report Project Team): "During the August 25 MEAC Teams meeting, Maycee Crofts,22 a Senior Field Representative for TPUSA, participated at the request of TPUSA."
  - S-12 p. 70 (report_finding, Utah Valley University After-Action Report (Project Team)): "The Major Events Assessment Committee (MEAC) convened on August 25 via Microsoft Teams, during which University and UVUPD stakeholders discussed the event and engaged with TPUSA representative Maycee Crofts."
  - S-12 p. 42 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report Project Team): "On September 2, he emailed a staff member in the University’s Clubs Office seeking contact information for TPUSA’s security representative because no member of the TPUSA security team had contacted him"

### C-13549 (ep 157)
- **Claim:** The host reads that on September 3, seven days before the event, Dan Flood and Chief Long spoke for the first time on a call lasting five minutes and 28 seconds, during which Flood said TPUSA typically has eight to ten security personnel.
- **Record:** S-12 (pp. 19, 42) says on September 3 Long and Dan Flood held the first security coordination call (5 minutes 28 seconds), in which Flood said TPUSA typically used 8-10 security personnel.
  - S-12 p. 19 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report (Lake Forest Group / Brownstein Hyatt Farber Schreck)): "Chief Long held the first security coordination call (5 minutes, 28 seconds) with Dan Flood, TPUSA Security Supervisor."
  - S-12 p. 19 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report (Lake Forest Group / Brownstein Hyatt Farber Schreck)): "Discussion focused on arrival, departure, and parking logistics. Flood indicated TPUSA typically used 8-10 security personnel."
  - S-12 p. 42 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report Project Team): "On September 3, Chief Long received a call from Dan Flood, TPUSA Security Supervisor."

### C-13550 (ep 157)
- **Claim:** The host reads that on the first call Dan Flood focused on arrival, departure and parking logistics, including a clean exit for Charlie Kirk's SUV.

  - S-12 p. 19 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report (Lake Forest Group / Brownstein Hyatt Farber Schreck)): "Discussion focused on arrival, departure, and parking logistics. Flood indicated TPUSA typically used 8-10 security personnel."

### C-13551 (ep 157)
- **Claim:** Per the review timeline the host reads, on September 3 (T-minus 7 days) Dan Flood told Chief Long that the national office was handling ticketing and ticket totals were not available; the host says the last figure Long had from Turning Point National was 600 people, so he could not prepare for the crowd.
- **Record:** S-12 (pp. 19, 42) says on September 3 Flood confirmed TPUSA's national office in Phoenix handled ticketing and ticket totals were not available, so Long could not obtain crowd estimates.
  - S-12 p. 19 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report (Lake Forest Group / Brownstein Hyatt Farber Schreck)): "Follow-up call (48 seconds) between Chief Long and Flood regarding ticketing and event schedule. Flood confirmed TPUSA’s national office handled ticketing."
  - S-12 p. 42 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report Project Team): "Flood confirmed that TPUSA’s national office in Phoenix was handling ticketing so TPUSA could gauge how many people planned to attend."
  - S-12 p. 19 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report (Lake Forest Group / Brownstein Hyatt Farber Schreck)): "Ticket totals were not available, and thus Chief Long was unable to obtain crowd estimates from the TPUSA ticketing process at that time."

  - S-12 p. 43 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report Project Team): "On September 8, at approximately 3:12 p.m., Dan Flood sent Chief Long a text message regarding the Hall of Flags outdoor pedestrian walkway located above the canopy area where Charlie Kirk would be speaking."

  - S-12 p. 43 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report Project Team): "Chief Long also contacted SIAC by e-mail at approximately 7:30 a.m. on September 10 to share an anonymous tip UVU received regarding a potential threat involving an electronic-magnetic-pulse device"

  - S-12 p. 43 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report Project Team): "At approximately 11:00 a.m., Flood texted Chief Long again regarding individuals on the Hall of Flags outdoor pedestrian walkway above the canopy where Charlie Kirk would be speaking and requested that the area be taped off and secured."

  - S-12 p. 14 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report (Lake Forest Group / Brownstein Hyatt Farber Schreck)): "Chief Long texted Dan Flood to meet on the Hall of Flags outdoor pedestrian walkway at 11, however this face-to-face meeting did not occur."
  - S-12 p. 44 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report Project Team): "Chief Long texted Dan Flood to meet on the Hall of Flags outdoor pedestrian walkway at 11:00 a.m., however this face-to-face meeting did not occur."

### C-13566 (ep 157)

  - S-12 p. 48 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report Project Team): "multiple interviews the Project Team conducted indicated that UVU had expressed concerns about the outdoor location, including during the August 25 MEAC meeting, but ultimately agreed to TPUSA’s requested venue location when TPUSA representative Maycee Crofts insisted on it."

- **Claim:** The host reads that Harpole said that on arrival a few hours before the event his team surveyed the area and believed the venue location was horrible, and described an elevated 180 degree view as a security fail while acknowledging Kirk liked the venue.
- **Record:** S-12 (p. 47) says Harpole described arriving hours early, finding the venue 'horrible', an elevated 180-degree view, 'a security fail', and that Kirk 'liked' the venue.
  - S-12 p. 47 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report Project Team): "when he and his team arrived at UVU a few hours before the event, they surveyed the area and believed the venue location was “horrible.”"
  - S-12 p. 47 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report Project Team): "someone in an elevated position would have a 180-degree view of the area where Charlie Kirk would be speaking."
  - S-12 p. 47 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report Project Team): "Harpole described this as “ignorance” and “a security fail” but he acknowledged that Charlie Kirk “liked” the venue."

### C-13580 (ep 157)
- **Claim:** Brian Harpole stated on Shawn Ryan’s podcast that UVU selected the venue and told TPUSA where the event had to be held; the independent review’s interviews indicate UVU expressed concerns about the outdoor location and that TPUSA pushed the courtyard site.
- **Record:** S-12 (p. 48) says Harpole stated UVU selected the venue, while interviews indicated UVU expressed concerns but agreed when TPUSA's Crofts insisted on the location.
  - S-12 p. 48 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report Project Team): "Harpole further stated that the venue location was selected by UVU and that UVU told TPUSA, “Nope, this is where you have to have it.”"
  - S-12 p. 48 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report Project Team): "multiple interviews the Project Team conducted indicated that UVU had expressed concerns about the outdoor location, including during the August 25 MEAC meeting, but ultimately agreed to TPUSA’s requested venue location when TPUSA representative Maycee Crofts insisted on it."

### C-13581 (ep 157)
- **Claim:** The host reads that Harpole said TPUSA's video team used what he described as little bitty drones to take a quick shot of the venue before the event but did not use drones during the event because of FAA restrictions tied to Provo airspace.
- **Record:** S-12 (p. 48) says Harpole stated the TPUSA video team used a 'little-bitty' drone for a pre-event shot but did not fly drones during the event due to Provo Airport FAA restrictions.
  - S-12 p. 48 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report Project Team): "He stated that the TPUSA “video team” used what he described as a “little- bitty” drone to take a quick shot of the venue before the event."
  - S-12 p. 48 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report Project Team): "Harpole also stated that although his team had drones, they did not use them during the event because of FAA restrictions related to Provo Airport airspace."

### C-13583 (ep 157)
- **Claim:** The host reads the review's statement that if the team had concerns about the venue, a drone from the neighboring Orem Police Department could have been requested, and the host says it was not requested.
- **Record:** S-12 (pp. 48, 53) says an Orem Police drone could have been requested and Chief Robinson would have provided it had it been requested.
  - S-12 p. 48 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report Project Team): "A drone from the neighboring Orem Police Department could have been requested."
  - S-12 p. 53 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report Project Team): "Chief B.J. Robinson advised that the Orem Police Department has aerial drone capability that can provide live video footage. He would have provided that resource to Chief Long had it been requested"

### C-13584 (ep 157)
- **Claim:** The host reads that Officer Bagley was the first to suggest going up to the Losee Center after the shot and identified the elevated roof as a possible source location.
- **Record:** S-12 (p. 49) says Officer Chris Bagley identified the elevated Losee Center roof as a possible source location of the assailant.
  - S-12 p. 49 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report Project Team): "Officer Chris Bagley identified the elevated roof area of the Losee Center as a possible source location of the assailant."

### C-13585 (ep 157)
- **Claim:** The host reads that Bagley located a screwdriver and an area of disturbed gravel on the roof that appeared consistent with a possible prone shooting position, which helped focus the investigation on the roof.
- **Record:** S-12 (p. 49) says Bagley located a screwdriver and disturbed gravel on the roof consistent with a possible prone shooting position.
  - S-12 p. 49 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report Project Team): "Officer Bagley went to that area, where he located a screwdriver and an area of disturbed gravel on the roof that appeared consistent with a possible prone shooting position."

### C-13588 (ep 157)
- **Claim:** The host reads the review's finding that there was limited time between Chief Long first learning of the event and the event date, and that the security assessment did not result in documented venue modification requirements.
- **Record:** S-12 (p. 54) says the Security Assessment process did not result in documented venue-modification requirements.
  - S-12 p. 54 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report Project Team): "The Security Assessment process did not result in documented requirements for venue modification, University-controlled ticketing, controlled access, credentialing, screening, or additional staffing."

### C-13589 (ep 157)
- **Claim:** The host reads that coordination with the TPUSA security team was very limited and did not include a structured pre-event briefing or site walk-through on or before September 10 between Harpole, Flood and UVUPD.
- **Record:** S-12 (pp. 44, 64) says coordination with TPUSA security was limited and there was no formal pre-event briefing or walk-through with Harpole, Flood and UVUPD.
  - S-12 p. 44 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report Project Team): "The absence of a formal pre-event briefing and coordinated walk-through with Brian Harpole, Dan Flood, and the TPUSA security team was a critical missed opportunity."
  - S-12 p. 64 (report_finding, Utah Valley University After-Action Report (Project Team)): "Coordination with TPUSA’s security team was also limited."
  - S-12 p. 64 (report_finding, Utah Valley University After-Action Report (Project Team)): "UVUPD, TPUSA’s security team, emergency management, and event organizers did not conduct a formal pre-event briefing and walk-through focused on line-of-sight concerns, elevated positions, arrival and departure routes, building access"

### C-13590 (ep 157)
- **Claim:** The host reads the review's finding that there was no dedicated assignment for real-time security video monitoring by UVUPD dispatch or others from another location during the event.
- **Record:** S-12 (p. 54) says there was no dedicated assignment for real-time security video monitoring by UVUPD Dispatch or others from another location during the event.
  - S-12 p. 54 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report Project Team): "There also was not a dedicated assignment for real-time security video monitoring by UVUPD Dispatch personnel or others from another operational location during the event."

### C-13591 (ep 157)
- **Claim:** The host reads from the review that TPUSA never actually issued tickets, and says attendees instead registered for the event, handled by TPUSA National and not communicated to police.
- **Record:** S-12 (p. 61) says TPUSA never issued tickets to registrants and registration was managed through TPUSA's national office rather than UVU's ticketing system.
  - S-12 p. 61 (report_finding, Utah Valley University After-Action Report (Project Team)): "The Project Team noted that TPUSA never issued tickets to those who registered for the event, nor did they require tickets at the actual event."
  - S-12 p. 61 (report_finding, Utah Valley University After-Action Report (Project Team)): "Registration was managed by TPUSA through its national office rather than through UVU’s ticketing system."

### C-13592 (ep 157)
- **Claim:** The host reads that the review states there was no formal credentialing process for VIPs, no ticket verification, no screening, no designated entry and exit points and no restricted access areas.
- **Record:** S-12 (p. 62) says no formal credentialing process was used (including for VIPs) and no formal attendee screening or controlled perimeter was established.
  - S-12 p. 62 (report_finding, Utah Valley University After-Action Report (Project Team)): "No formal credentialing process was used to differentiate event participants, TPUSA personnel, University staff, vendors, media representatives, VIPs, emergency responders, or other authorized personnel."
  - S-12 p. 62 (report_finding, Utah Valley University After-Action Report (Project Team)): "No formal attendee screening operation was established at entry points. Attendees generally accessed the event area without passing through a controlled security perimeter."

### C-13619 (ep 157)
- **Claim:** The host says UVU police asked Maycee Crofts for a TPUSA security contact after the August 25 meeting and none was provided, so Chief Long went back to Caleb and asked again on September 2.
- **Record:** S-12 (pp. 19, 42) says Long gave his contact to Crofts to pass to the appropriate TPUSA representative, and on September 2 advised no security request had been received from TPUSA and sought a point of contact.
  - S-12 p. 42 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report Project Team): "Chief Long provided his contact information to Maycee Crofts and asked that she share it with the appropriate TPUSA representative"
  - S-12 p. 19 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report (Lake Forest Group / Brownstein Hyatt Farber Schreck)): "September 2 Chief Long advised no security request had been received from TPUSA and sought a point of contact."

### C-13629 (ep 157)
- **Claim:** The host says TPUSA officials were willing to speak to the press including CBS News and ABC News and official spokespersons, but not to sit down with an independent review, even though she says they could have required employee Dan Flood to.
- **Record:** The After-Action Report says neither Dan Flood nor Maycee Crofts answered the Project Team's calls or voicemails, and TPUSA senior staff in Phoenix did not answer requests to connect them (S-12 p. 8); the press-interview part is not addressed.
  - S-12 p. 8 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report (Lake Forest Group / Brownstein Hyatt Farber Schreck)): "Neither Flood nor Crofts responded to phone calls or voicemail messages."
  - S-12 p. 8 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report (Lake Forest Group / Brownstein Hyatt Farber Schreck)): "The Project Team further contacted senior-level staff members at TPUSA in Phoenix seeking assistance in connecting with Dan Flood and Maycee Crofts. The Project Team did not receive a response"

### C-13783 (ep 159)
- **Claim:** The host says that on July 18 a formal request from Turning Point USA's UVU chapter was submitted, and the decision was made to hold the event at Utah Valley University.
- **Record:** S-12 timeline (p. 18) says a formal event request for a Charlie Kirk appearance was submitted by the UVU TPUSA Club on July 18.
  - S-12 p. 18 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report (Lake Forest Group / Brownstein Hyatt Farber Schreck)): "July 18 Formal event request submitted by UVU TPUSA Club. Event described as an appearance by Charlie Kirk to discuss current events. External ticketing by TPUSA anticipated."

### C-13806 (ep 159)
- **Claim:** Citing the independent review, the host says that on the August 25 UVU major events Teams meeting TPUSA insisted on the venue it wanted, TPUSA security was not on the call, and UVU police chief Jeff Long asked for a security contact and did not get one.
- **Record:** S-12 (pp. 18, 42) says at the August 25 MEAC meeting Chief Long requested contact with TPUSA security leadership, and by September 2 no member of the TPUSA security team had contacted him.
  - S-12 p. 18 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report (Lake Forest Group / Brownstein Hyatt Farber Schreck)): "TPUSA advised UVU it would have approximately eight security personnel. Chief Long requested contact with TPUSA security leadership."
  - S-12 p. 42 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report Project Team): "On September 2, he emailed a staff member in the University’s Clubs Office seeking contact information for TPUSA’s security representative because no member of the TPUSA security team had contacted him"

### C-13807 (ep 159)
- **Claim:** The host says September 2 is the first time she could find the upcoming event mentioned, with Charlie to speak at UVU in the courtyard, that Chief Long then followed up with the club president and was given a contact, and that on September 3 he established a connection with Dan Flood.
- **Record:** S-12 (pp. 19, 42) says on September 3 Dan Flood called Chief Long, the first security coordination call; the host's September 2 point reflects her own search and the record names a Clubs Office staff member, not the club president.
  - S-12 p. 42 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report Project Team): "On September 3, Chief Long received a call from Dan Flood, TPUSA Security Supervisor."
  - S-12 p. 19 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report (Lake Forest Group / Brownstein Hyatt Farber Schreck)): "Chief Long held the first security coordination call (5 minutes, 28 seconds) with Dan Flood, TPUSA Security Supervisor."

### C-13855 (ep 160)
- **Claim:** The host says Brian Harpole said CPR was not administered in the car "or anywhere else for that matter", while the UVU review says a fire captain assisted with CPR at the hospital.
- **Record:** S-12 (pp. 22, 52) says the Station Two fire captain assisted in providing CPR to Charlie Kirk at Timpanogos Hospital (Harpole's remark itself is not in the record).
  - S-12 p. 52 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report Project Team): "Station Two then rerouted to Timpanogos Hospital, where the Fire Captain from Station Two assisted in providing CPR to Charlie Kirk at the hospital."
  - S-12 p. 22 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report (Lake Forest Group / Brownstein Hyatt Farber Schreck)): "Station Two then rerouted to Timpanogos Hospital, where a fire captain assisted with CPR."

### C-13858 (ep 160)
- **Claim:** The host reads the UVU independent review stating that Station 3 personnel radioed that their truck had passed near the TPUSA security team's SUV, Station 2 rerouted to Timpanogos Hospital, and the fire captain from Station 2 assisted in providing CPR to Charlie Kirk at the hospital; she flags that this public-figure captain is unnamed while the review named Caleb Chilcutt and Maycee Crofts.
- **Record:** S-12 (pp. 22, 52) says Station Three personnel passed the TPUSA security SUV and radioed it in, and Station Two rerouted to Timpanogos Hospital where its fire captain assisted with CPR.
  - S-12 p. 22 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report (Lake Forest Group / Brownstein Hyatt Farber Schreck)): "Station Three fire personnel passed the SUV being used by the TPUSA security team to transport the victim to Timpanogos Hospital and relayed that information by radio."
  - S-12 p. 52 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report Project Team): "Station Two then rerouted to Timpanogos Hospital, where the Fire Captain from Station Two assisted in providing CPR to Charlie Kirk at the hospital."

### C-13864 (ep 160)
- **Claim:** The host says the security team did not even try to get in touch with the police and that the police had to play offense.
- **Record:** The After-Action Report finds that on September 2 Chief Long had received no security request from TPUSA, and he emailed the Clubs Office for TPUSA security contact information because no TPUSA security member had contacted him (S-12 p. 42, p. 19).
  - S-12 p. 42 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report Project Team): "On September 2, he emailed a staff member in the University’s Clubs Office seeking contact information for TPUSA’s security representative because no member of the TPUSA security team had contacted him"
  - S-12 p. 19 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report (Lake Forest Group / Brownstein Hyatt Farber Schreck)): "September 2 Chief Long advised no security request had been received from TPUSA and sought a point of contact."

### C-13870 (ep 160)
- **Claim:** In the clip, Frank Turek says that coming into the venue he told Charlie he did not like the place because there were too many buildings.
- **Record:** Fox News (S-27) reports Turek said he told Kirk he was concerned about the large number of nearby buildings and the safety risk they posed.
  - S-27 para 163 (tier2_attributed, Frank Turek): "Turek told Kirk he was concerned about the large number of nearby buildings and the safety risk they could pose"

### C-13899 (ep 161)
- **Claim:** The host says that, as far as she knows, Discord is the place where Tyler Robinson published his alleged confession, and that she initially thought TPUSA surely would not use it against its enemies.
- **Record:** The bindover ruling (S-7, 08:10:31) relies on a message from Robinson's Discord account stating 'It was me at UVU yesterday.'
  - S-7 (08:10:31) (ruling, Judge Tony Graf Jr.): "These electronic statements are further corroborated by the alleged in-person confirmation to Twiggs the following morning and subsequent message from defendant's Discord account stating, "It was me at UVU yesterday.""

### C-14013 (ep 162)
- **Claim:** In a clip the host plays from the Shawn Ryan interview, Brian Harpole says they have correspondence with the school's police chief from the Monday before Charlie was killed and that the chief will not stand up like a man.
- **Record:** The Salt Lake Tribune (S-45) reports Harpole showed on The Shawn Ryan Show texts he exchanged with Chief Long two days before the Wednesday event, which was the Monday; the 'stand up like a man' remark is not covered.
  - S-45 para 10 (tier2_attributed, Brian Harpole): "Two days before Charlie Kirk was set to speak at Utah Valley University , the head of his contracted security team texted the school’s police chief."
  - S-45 para 3 (tier2_attributed, Brian Harpole): "Brian Harpole spoke on “The Shawn Ryan Show” and showed text messages he apparently exchanged with Chief Jeff Long days before Kirk’s shooting death."

### C-14014 (ep 162)
- **Claim:** In the clip, Harpole reads a message to the chief saying there is student roof access close to where Charlie would be set up, describing staircases at the Sorensen Center.
- **Record:** S-12 exhibit text (p. 43) reads that there is student roof access close to where CK will be set up, via Sorensen Student Center staircases to the roof walkways.
  - S-12 p. 43 (exhibit, Dan Flood, TPUSA Security Supervisor (text to Chief Long, Sept 8, 2025)): "there is student roof access pretty close to where CK will be set up at Utah Valley (The Sorensen Student center has a couple stair cases that go up to the walkways on the roof)"

### C-14015 (ep 162)
- **Claim:** In the clip, Harpole says the chief's last message was "I got you covered", asking "What else am I to do?"
- **Record:** The After-Action Report reproduces Chief Long's September 8 text to Dan Flood, 'I got you covered', replying to the request about the area above the speaker (S-12 p. 43).
  - S-12 p. 43 (exhibit, Chief Long (text to Dan Flood, Sept 8, 2025)): "Chief Long: I got you covered"

### C-14021 (ep 162)
- **Claim:** In a clip the host plays, introduced as Andrew Kolvet, the speaker reads the TPUSA security text to "Chief Long" and his reply "I got you covered", saying that was obviously not the case and asking why the rooftop was left unguarded.
- **Record:** S-12 (p. 43) shows Chief Long's text reply 'I got you covered' to TPUSA security and reports he advised the area above the canopy would be covered (the speaker's 'obviously not the case' is opinion).
  - S-12 p. 43 (exhibit, Chief Long (text to Dan Flood, Sept 8, 2025)): "Chief Long: I got you covered"
  - S-12 p. 43 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report Project Team): "Chief Long advised that the area above the canopy would be covered for security purposes."

### C-14025 (ep 162)
- **Claim:** The host says Jeff Long did a lot to correct course on September 10 and called in more officers when he realized more people were attending, which she says the report reflects.
- **Record:** The Salt Lake Tribune (S-46), reporting the independent review, says Long first assigned himself and two officers and by 11 a.m. asked three more to join because of the crowd size; 'did a lot to correct course' is the host's characterisation.
  - S-46 para 61 (tier2_attributed, UVU independent review (Brownstein Hyatt Farber Schreck / The Lake Forest Group)): "Initially, Long assigned only himself and two others to patrol the event. By 11 a.m. though — an hour before Kirk was set to appear — the crowd size prompted him to ask three more officers to join"

### C-14026 (ep 162)
- **Claim:** The host says Brian Harpole is suing her while, she alleges, defaming someone else.
- **Record:** The New York Times (S-66) reports Harpole sued Candace Owens for defamation and is also suing Mitch Snow; the host's allegation that he defames someone else is not assessed.
  - S-66 para 9 (tier2_reported, The New York Times): "The former head of security for Charlie Kirk sued podcaster Candace Owens on Thursday for defamation, claiming that she falsely implicated him in a conspiracy to assassinate Kirk."
  - S-66 para 10 (tier2_reported, The New York Times): "He is also suing Mitch Snow, a retired Army sergeant who stated on Owens’ podcast that he was an eyewitness to what he described as the secret encounter."

### C-14028 (ep 162)
- **Claim:** The host says Robin Ebmeyer is or was UVU's emergency management director.
- **Record:** S-12 (p. 74) identifies Robin Ebmeyer as UVU's Director of Emergency Management and Safety.
  - S-12 p. 74 (report_finding, Utah Valley University After-Action Report (Project Team)): "The response was also affected by the physical absence of Robin Ebmeyer, UVU’s Director of Emergency Management and Safety, who was out of state at the time of the incident."

### C-14029 (ep 162)
- **Claim:** The host says the independent review states Robin Ebmeyer was present at the major events committee meeting where Maycee Crofts insisted on holding the event outside, and that Ebmeyer expressed it was not a good idea.
- **Record:** S-12 (pp. 41, 48) says Ebmeyer was among MEAC members who expressed reservations, and UVU voiced outdoor-location concerns at the August 25 MEAC meeting before agreeing when Crofts insisted.
  - S-12 p. 41 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report Project Team): "Some MEAC members continued to express reservations, including Robin Ebmeyer, UVU’s Director of Emergency Management and Safety."
  - S-12 p. 48 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report Project Team): "multiple interviews the Project Team conducted indicated that UVU had expressed concerns about the outdoor location, including during the August 25 MEAC meeting, but ultimately agreed to TPUSA’s requested venue location when TPUSA representative Maycee Crofts insisted on it."

### C-14030 (ep 162)
- **Claim:** Per the independent review passage the host reads, Crofts insisted on the fountain courtyard because Charlie had spoken on campus before and TPUSA knew the location, and preferred TPUSA manage online ticketing through its Phoenix national office.
- **Record:** S-12 (p. 41) says Crofts insisted on the Fountain Courtyard because Kirk had previously spoken on campus, and preferred TPUSA's Phoenix national office manage online ticketing.
  - S-12 p. 41 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report Project Team): "Crofts insisted on the use of the Fountain Courtyard location because Charlie Kirk had previously spoken on campus and TPUSA was familiar with that location."
  - S-12 p. 41 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report Project Team): "Crofts also reportedly preferred that TPUSA manage online ticketing through its national office in Phoenix."

### C-14031 (ep 162)
- **Claim:** Per the review passage the host reads, some committee members continued to express reservations, including Robin Ebmeyer, UVU's director of emergency management and safety.
- **Record:** S-12 (p. 41) says some MEAC members continued to express reservations, including Robin Ebmeyer, UVU's Director of Emergency Management and Safety.
  - S-12 p. 41 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report Project Team): "Some MEAC members continued to express reservations, including Robin Ebmeyer, UVU’s Director of Emergency Management and Safety."

### C-14032 (ep 162)
- **Claim:** The host says page 73 of the review states Ebmeyer was absent from the university on September 10 on a pre-scheduled vacation, which the review said could have contributed to confusion in the emergency response.
- **Record:** The UVU After-Action Report (S-12 p. 52) says Emergency Management Director Robin Ebmeyer was scheduled to be out of state on vacation that day, and p. 74 says the response was affected by her physical absence.
  - S-12 p. 52 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report Project Team): "UVU Emergency Management Director Robin Ebmeyer was scheduled to be out of state on vacation that day."
  - S-12 p. 74 (report_finding, Utah Valley University After-Action Report (Project Team)): "The response was also affected by the physical absence of Robin Ebmeyer, UVU’s Director of Emergency Management and Safety, who was out of state at the time of the incident."

### C-14103 (ep 163)
- **Claim:** Per the book excerpt, Charlie's security team in the SUV was Justin, Dan, Brian and Rick.
- **Record:** KSL (S-58) reports Harpole's account placing driver Justin, Dan Flood, Rick Cutler and Harpole himself in the vehicle with Kirk, matching the four names in the excerpt.
  - S-58 para 19 (tier2_attributed, Brian Harpole): "We didn’t even attempt to close it. I just told Justin, ‘Go, go, go.’ Dan’s already got the route done,” Harpole said."
  - S-58 para 19 (tier2_attributed, Brian Harpole): "another private security operator, Rick Cutler, held onto Harpole’s shirt to keep him from falling out of the van"
  - S-58 para 19 (tier2_attributed, Brian Harpole): "Several members of Harpole’s team, Dan Flood, Turning Point USA’s security director, and Frank Turek, a Christian author and apologist close to Kirk, jumped in the van."

### C-14108 (ep 163)
- **Claim:** In a clip the host plays from the Shawn Ryan show, Brian Harpole says Charlie's left leg was down in the door so it would not close, and he was on his knees with the door open "doing the medical".
- **Record:** The Salt Lake Tribune (S-59) reports Harpole saying Kirk was so tall that the car doors could not close while he lay in the back seat, and KSL (S-58) reports they drove off without closing it; the left-leg detail is not covered.
  - S-59 para 24 (tier2_attributed, Brian Harpole): "Harpole recalled that Kirk was so tall — he was 6-foot-5 — that when he was lying in the backseat, they couldn’t close the car doors."
  - S-58 para 19 (tier2_attributed, Brian Harpole): "We didn’t even attempt to close it. I just told Justin, ‘Go, go, go.’ Dan’s already got the route done,” Harpole said."

### C-14109 (ep 163)
- **Claim:** In the clip, Brian Harpole says Justin was driving at 60, 80, 100 and Rick held him across the kit and his shirt so he could use both hands.
- **Record:** KSL (S-58) reports Harpole saying he told Justin to go and that Rick Cutler held onto his shirt so he would not fall out of the van; the speeds are not covered.
  - S-58 para 19 (tier2_attributed, Brian Harpole): "another private security operator, Rick Cutler, held onto Harpole’s shirt to keep him from falling out of the van"
  - S-58 para 19 (tier2_attributed, Brian Harpole): "We didn’t even attempt to close it. I just told Justin, ‘Go, go, go.’ Dan’s already got the route done,” Harpole said."

### C-14110 (ep 163)
- **Claim:** In the clip, Brian Harpole says he was working on the medical while Frank and Rick prayed out loud; the host asks when Turek tried to stop the bleeding.
- **Record:** KSL (S-58) and The Salt Lake Tribune (S-59) report Harpole saying Turek prayed out loud while Harpole worked to stop the bleeding; Rick praying is not covered.
  - S-58 para 19 (tier2_attributed, Brian Harpole): "Meanwhile, Turek prayed out loud."
  - S-59 para 25 (tier2_attributed, Brian Harpole): "They drove anyway, with Harpole using 36 feet of dressing, he said, to try to stop the bleeding."

### C-14116 (ep 163)
- **Claim:** The host says she remembers learning that it was Brian Harpole who found the room, cut the shirt and got Charlie ready for the defibrillator, then escorted himself out.
- **Record:** KSL (S-58) reports Harpole saying he cut off Kirk's shirt to prep him for a defibrillator; finding the room and leaving on his own are not covered.
  - S-58 para 21 (tier2_attributed, Brian Harpole): "Then Harpole said he cut Kirk’s Freedom T-shirt off to prep him for a defibrillator."

### C-14161 (ep 164)
- **Claim:** The host says Val Peterson was not at the university on September 10 when Charlie Kirk was assassinated.
- **Record:** S-12 p. 74 states that at the time of the shooting Peterson was approximately 45 minutes from campus and joined by speakerphone while traveling to UVU.
  - S-12 p. 74 (report_finding, Utah Valley University After-Action Report (Project Team)): "At the time of the shooting, Peterson was approximately 45 minutes from campus. He joined response discussions by speakerphone while traveling to UVU and remained actively engaged."

### C-14162 (ep 164)
- **Claim:** Per the after-action report the host shows, Peterson was approximately 45 minutes from campus at the time of the shooting and joined response discussions by speakerphone while traveling to UVU.
- **Record:** S-12 p. 74 states that Peterson was approximately 45 minutes from campus at the time of the shooting and joined response discussions by speakerphone while traveling to UVU.
  - S-12 p. 74 (report_finding, Utah Valley University After-Action Report (Project Team)): "At the time of the shooting, Peterson was approximately 45 minutes from campus. He joined response discussions by speakerphone while traveling to UVU and remained actively engaged."

### C-14185 (ep 164)
- **Claim:** In the clip, Turek says his computer bag, wallet and passport were in the car, that Charlie Kirk had been lying on the bag across the back seat, and that it was full of blood.
- **Record:** Fox News (S-27) reports Turek said Kirk was lying on Turek's computer bag as the team drove him to the hospital; the wallet, passport and blood details are not covered.
  - S-27 para 185 (tier2_attributed, Frank Turek): "Turek found the cross draped over his own computer bag, which Kirk was lying on as the team drove him to the hospital."

### C-14186 (ep 164)
- **Claim:** In the clip, Turek says that as he pulled out the bag, Charlie Kirk's necklace, which they had been looking for and which Erika Kirk had asked about, was across the top of it.
- **Record:** Fox News (S-27) reports Turek said Erika asked for her husband's missing cross necklace and he found it draped over his computer bag.
  - S-27 para 184 (tier2_attributed, Frank Turek): "Turek said Erika asked for her husband's cross necklace, which went missing during the rush."
  - S-27 para 185 (tier2_attributed, Frank Turek): "Turek found the cross draped over his own computer bag, which Kirk was lying on as the team drove him to the hospital."

### C-14235 (ep 165)
- **Claim:** The host says the independent review report states Val Peterson was 45 minutes away on September 10.
- **Record:** The independent After-Action Report (S-12 p. 74) states that Peterson was approximately 45 minutes from campus at the time of the shooting.
  - S-12 p. 74 (report_finding, Utah Valley University After-Action Report (Project Team)): "At the time of the shooting, Peterson was approximately 45 minutes from campus. He joined response discussions by speakerphone while traveling to UVU and remained actively engaged."

### C-14254 (ep 165)
- **Claim:** The host says that, according to the UVU review, Maycee Crofts was on the call and wanted the event in the amphitheater, and links her to the Meridian area.
- **Record:** S-12 p. 40 says TPUSA's Maycee Crofts took part in the August 25 MEAC Teams meeting, and p. 48 says UVU agreed to TPUSA's requested outdoor venue when Crofts insisted on it; the Meridian link is not addressed.
  - S-12 p. 40 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report Project Team): "During the August 25 MEAC Teams meeting, Maycee Crofts,22 a Senior Field Representative for TPUSA, participated at the request of TPUSA."
  - S-12 p. 48 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report Project Team): "multiple interviews the Project Team conducted indicated that UVU had expressed concerns about the outdoor location, including during the August 25 MEAC meeting, but ultimately agreed to TPUSA’s requested venue location when TPUSA representative Maycee Crofts insisted on it."

### C-14317 (ep 166)
- **Claim:** The host says that, according to Turek, Charlie Kirk's body had been on top of the bag in the car during the ride to the hospital.
- **Record:** Fox News (S-27) reports Turek said Kirk was lying on his computer bag as the team drove him to the hospital.
  - S-27 para 185 (tier2_attributed, Frank Turek): "Turek found the cross draped over his own computer bag, which Kirk was lying on as the team drove him to the hospital."

### C-14393 (ep 166)
- **Claim:** The host says that, according to the UVU Independent Review, a fire captain performed CPR on Charlie when he arrived at the hospital, and that she wants to know who it was.
- **Record:** S-12 p. 52 states that the Fire Captain from Station Two helped give Charlie Kirk CPR at Timpanogos Hospital (also p. 22).
  - S-12 p. 52 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report Project Team): "Station Two then rerouted to Timpanogos Hospital, where the Fire Captain from Station Two assisted in providing CPR to Charlie Kirk at the hospital."
  - S-12 p. 22 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report (Lake Forest Group / Brownstein Hyatt Farber Schreck)): "Station Two then rerouted to Timpanogos Hospital, where a fire captain assisted with CPR."

### C-14394 (ep 166)
- **Claim:** The host says the reports indicate the captain was from fire station two, "I believe", and that she thinks it was in Orem, though maybe Provo.
- **Record:** S-12 p. 52 identifies the captain who helped with CPR at Timpanogos Hospital as the Fire Captain from Orem Fire Station Two.
  - S-12 p. 52 (report_finding, UVU After-Action Report Project Team): "Station Two then rerouted to Timpanogos Hospital, where the Fire Captain from Station Two assisted in providing CPR to Charlie Kirk at the hospital."
